# Decision log

One entry per decision or departure from the books: date, stage, decision, reason, books affected, approved by.

## 2026-11-10, Stage 0 decisions (approved by the user)
| # | Decision | Decided |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Engine and language | TypeScript (strict) with WebGL2; a new project built from scratch |
| 2 | Platforms and input | Desktop browser first; keyboard, mouse and gamepad |
| 3 | Art production | Final art authored by Claude as palette-indexed sources, compiled and visually checked; the user reviews at gates |
| 4 | Art scale | 640 x 360 viewport, 24 px tiles, 32 x 40 people; all art new |
| 5 | Combat model | Real time, no pause: light attack, charged heavy, parry, block, dodge, stamina, facing |
| 6 | Player character | The player names the steerer and chooses one of two body models; gender never stated |
| 7 | Steerer death | Yes: game over, with reload; optional Second wind |
| 8 | Crew permadeath | Yes, with the Rán's mercy option |
| 9 | Campaign length | OPEN. Provisional 40 to 60 hours over 270 days, days of about 17 minutes; confirm after Build 2 |
| 10 | Sea map | Charted routes on a node graph |
| 11 | Sea battles | One-on-one first; up to three ships late; no fleet command |
| 12 | Violence | Mature: heavy blood and gore; civilians and the surrendered can be killed, with consequences from witnesses and evidence. Hard limits: no children as actors, no sexual violence, no player torture |
| 13 | Myths | Ambiguous: powers seen through signs and proxies; relics never settled |
| 14 | Language and names | Native-form names with accents; English dialogue with a curated glossary (Book 8) |
| 15 | Nessa's stance | Random per campaign, hidden |
| 16 | Saves | Free saves when safe, rotating autosaves, checkpoints |
| 17 | Romance | None; characters may have existing relationships |
| 18 | Authored versus procedural | Hand-authored scenarios and cast; generated residents and minor variants only |
| 19 | Audio | All audio generated by the game (no recordings, composer, voice acting or licensed music); voice blips, not voiced barks; music is a priority (Book 11 section 12). Changed from "Voiced barks at most" on 2026-11-10 (AU-1) |
| 20 | Gate sign-off and title | The user playtests each build gate. Title: Oar and Ember (no existing game found by web search; trademark check still needed) |

## 2026-11-10, further design decisions
- The home (Skarvholm, later the mainland home) is fully playable on foot, with tasks, building designation, conversations and combat. Books 1, 2, 4, 9, 10.
- A 120-day year of four seasons with consequences is the rhythm of a 270-day campaign. Books 1, 4, 10.
- Late game: the required Return to Skarvholm, held by Orm's garrison, to recover Manannán's Cup. Books 1, 4, 5, 6, 9, 10.
- No existing codebase: Stage 1 creates the project and publishes it to GitHub Pages. Books 2, 3.
- Open: who composes the final music (Book 11 section 11).

## 2026-11-10, Stage 1 (project setup)
Decisions taken while setting up the project. None changes a locked Book 1 feature. Approved by: pending the user's review.

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1-1 | Toolchain pinned: TypeScript 6.0.3, Vite 8.3.5, Vitest 5.1.0, ESLint 10.13.0 with typescript-eslint 8.72.1, Prettier 3.9.10, Playwright 1.63.0, Node 22 | typescript-eslint does not yet support TypeScript 7. Vitest 5.0.5 cannot be installed (npm's resolver crashes on its open-ended optional peer), so 5.1.0 is used. Playwright 1.63.0 matches the browser pre-installed in the build environment | TypeScript 7 without type-aware lint; Vitest 4 | 2 (section 01) |
| S1-2 | The palette's hex values are transcribed from the Book 7 PDF figure (section 08, page 9) into `assets/palette/book07.json`, with one-character codes for art sources | The markdown Book 7 says the palette is "drawn in the PDF version" and gives only the blood reds as text | Ask for the values to be added to the markdown first (recommended for the future: add the 24-colour table to Book 7 section 08) | 7 (sections 08, 11) |
| S1-3 | The title lettering is authored as glyph masks finished deterministically (Scale2x, one-pixel ink outline, three-tone ramp lit from the upper left); a named ramp maps the result to palette codes when text is drawn | One glyph must be drawn in two materials (the "r" of "Oar" in wool and of "Ember" in hearth colours), which rows of fixed palette codes cannot do. Every pixel is still a palette entry, and the outline and lighting follow Book 7 section 11 | Duplicate every glyph per colour | 2 (section 19), 7 (section 11) |
| S1-4 | Stage 1 compiles the two font sources when the page loads and composes the static title frame on the CPU; WebGL2 presents it at a whole-number scale | The art compiler, atlases and GPU sprite drawing are Stage 4 deliverables. The same deterministic code runs in the browser, the tests and the render-to-image check, so they agree pixel for pixel | Build the Stage 4 pipeline early | 2 (section 19), 3 (Stage 4) |
| S1-5 | Below 640 x 360 device pixels the scale stays at 1 and the frame is cropped evenly rather than shrunk; letterbox bars use Outline ink `#22313A` | The books require whole-number scaling and letterboxing but do not cover windows smaller than the frame or name a bar colour | Fractional down-scaling (would blur pixels) | 1 (section 17), 7 (section 01) |
| S1-6 | The build id is `<version>+<short commit>` (e.g. `0.1.0+9d16de8`), with version `0.<stage>.<patch>` | A playtest note can then always be matched to the exact code that ran | CI run number | 2 (section 20) |
| S1-7 | The Book 2 layer rules are enforced by lint (`no-restricted-imports`), and Node built-ins are allowed only in `src/tools` | Catches a forbidden dependency the moment it is written | Review by hand | 2 (section 02) |
| S1-8 | Added `tests/browser/` (Playwright smoke tests in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit) beside the Book 2 test folders | Acceptance check 2 needs a browser test, and the workflow repeats it against the published address | Manual checks only | 2 (sections 02, 23) |
| S1-9 | WebKit (Safari's engine, run by Playwright on Linux) stands in for Safari in automated checks; real Safari is checked by the user at the published address | GitHub's Linux runners cannot run Safari itself | macOS runners with safaridriver | 3 (Stage 1 check 2) |
| S1-10 | The ID registry is `content/ids.json`: 402 entries (91 stated in the books, 13 expanded from shorthands such as `MUS_HOME_DAWN/DAY/DUSK/NIGHT`, 298 assigned in Stage 1), 6 ID families and the 19 Book 6 deed tags. Each entry cites its book and section and quotes the book verbatim; tests check both. 65 candidate IDs the extraction marked as doubtful were held back for the stage that needs them | Stage 1 asks for a registry seeded from Books 4 to 11, but most named things in Books 6, 7, 8 and 10 have no written ID. Seeding only confident IDs protects the "stable forever" rule | Seed stated IDs only | 2 (section 04) |
| S1-11 | Registry naming: proper names join into one token as the books do (`FORT_DUNFIACH`); descriptive words are separated (`SK_WRECK_STRAND`). `REG_SK` is assigned for Skarvholm's on-foot region, because the stated `ZONE_SK_*` zones need a parent. The Keepers' faction is `FAC_KEEPERS`; the Return scenario is `SCN_RETURN`, with its complications `CMP_RETURN_*`. `MUS_THEME_*` leitmotifs are left out because they are material inside cues, not cues. Seven tasks cite Book 2 section 07 and `SCN_CARRICK` is stated in Book 2 section 23, both outside Books 4 to 11 | Consistency with the stated IDs; short, readable IDs | `FAC_INISRON`, `SCN_RETURN_SKARVHOLM` | 2 (section 04), 4, 5, 6, 11 |
| S1-12 | An assigned ID may be renamed, with a decision-log entry, only until content first uses it (Stage 3 onwards); after that it is frozen like a stated ID | IDs are stable forever, but nothing uses the assigned ones yet | Freeze all IDs now | 2 (section 04) |
| S1-13 | Interface strings use the speaker `ui` in Book 8 keys (e.g. `ui.title.name`) | Book 8 section 10 defines `<speaker>.<context>.<id>` but has no speaker for interface text | `sys` | 8 (section 10) |
| S1-14 | The Firefox smoke test sets the preference `webgl.force-enabled` | GitHub's runners have no GPU, and headless Firefox there turned WebGL off (the game correctly showed its "needs WebGL2" message). The preference makes Firefox use its software renderer. It changes only the test browser, not the game | Skip Firefox in CI | 3 (Stage 1 check 2) |
| S1-15 | The canvas size in device pixels is CSS size times the pixel ratio. The browser's exact device-pixel size is used only when it agrees within one pixel | Emulated (and some headless) browsers report CSS pixels as "device pixels". Trusting them made the browser stretch the canvas, which blurs the art at ratios such as 1.25 or 1.5. Found by the pixel-ratio-2 browser test | Trust the reported size | 1 (section 17), 7 (section 01) |
| S1-16 | Firefox and WebKit browser checks run on GitHub's macOS runners (Intel `macos-15-intel`, because arm64 `macos-latest` jobs were twice cancelled unassigned for lack of capacity); Chromium stays on Linux. Approved by the user (2026-11-10) | Twice on Linux runners, Firefox had WebGL2 turned off and WebKit returned black screenshots. macOS runners have a GPU and run WebKit much closer to real Safari | The user checks Firefox and Safari by hand | 3 (Stage 1 check 2) |
| S1-17 | While Stages 1 to 6 are built on `claude/lucid-bell-kgmdfi`, pushes to that branch also publish to GitHub Pages, so every stage is playable at the link before the single final pull request. Remove the branch from the workflow once it is merged. Approved by the user (2026-11-10) | Stage 1 check 2 needs a published address, and the user wants one pull request at the end | Publish from main only | 2 (section 01), 3 (Stage 1) |
| S1-18 | Publishing waits for Chromium (Linux) and Firefox (macOS) only. The WebKit check still runs on every push and against the published address, but does not block (`continue-on-error`). The game records its start-up steps on the canvas (`data-boot`, `data-error`) so the WebKit stall can be diagnosed. Approved by the user (2026-11-10) | WebKit failed three times in CI: black screenshots on Linux, and a stall before the title on macOS with no error. The user checks real Safari at the published link | Keep WebKit blocking; drop it | 3 (Stage 1 check 2) |

## 2026-11-10, Stage 2 (core runtime and calendar)
Approved by: pending the user's review (the user authorised Stages 1 to 6 to continue without waiting).

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S2-1 | Spring tides fall on days 1, 2, 3 and 16, 17, 18 of each season (Book 10), not days 1 and 16 (Book 2 section 03) | The books contradict each other. Book 10 is the only source of numbers (CLAUDE.md authority rule), and the user did not object when this was raised | Book 2's two days | 2 (section 03), 10 (section 02) |
| S2-2 | A calendar day runs from 06:00 to the next 06:00, so its watches are Morning, Day, Evening and Night (00:00 to 06:00). `minuteOfDay` is clock time after midnight | Book 10 lists the night watch as the day's fourth watch, and a day runs from waking to waking | Turn the day at midnight | 2 (section 03), 10 (section 02) |
| S2-3 | Low water falls where an ebb watch turns to flood. When the one-watch shift every three days puts two ebb watches together, they make one long ebb with a single low water at its end | Book 10 says "low water at the end of each ebb watch" and "1 watch every 3 days"; this reads both literally without two low waters six hours apart | Two low waters in a row | 2 (section 03), 10 (section 02) |
| S2-4 | The year (120 days) and season (30 days) lengths are calendar constants citing Book 1 section 12, and the 60 Hz tick is a constant citing Book 2 section 03; none is in `balance.json` | They are locked requirements and contracts, not tuning numbers, and Book 10 does not list them | Add them to balance.json (Stage 3 could, with the user's agreement) | 1, 2, 10 |
| S2-5 | Stage 2's simulation state holds only the calendar, the on-foot clock, the player's waking state and the RNG streams; tide is computed from the calendar rather than stored. Stage 3 grows it into Book 2's full WorldState | Stage 3 owns WorldState; derived values in saved state could drift from their rules | Store tide state | 2 (sections 03, 05) |
| S2-6 | The game minute advances in integer steps: each tick adds 100 units, and a minute costs secondsPerHour x day-length percent units. A refused command emits COMMAND_REFUSED with a reason code and the limit (for example 18:00) | Integer arithmetic keeps replays exact at every day-length option (0.75 gives 37.5 ticks per minute); reasons let the UI explain every refusal (Book 2 section 21) | Floating-point minutes | 2 (sections 03, 21) |
| S2-7 | The determinism lint rule also covers `src/core`, and bans Date, performance, timers and crypto randomness as well as Math.random | core holds the RNG and tick that sim builds on | sim only | 2 (sections 03, 24) |

## 2026-11-10, Stage 3 (content, world state and saves)
Approved by: pending the user's review (the user authorised Stages 1 to 6 to continue without waiting).

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S3-1 | Seeded content: Book 4's sea-map places, home sites, Skarvholm places, sea hazards, regions and Skarvholm zones; Book 5's 36 named people and 8 factions; Book 7 and 10's 4 hulls and 10 fittings. Tasks, buildings, scenarios, puzzles and dialogue are left to the stages that build them | Stage 3 asks for seed data from Books 4, 5, 7 and 10; content without its system would only drift | Seed every book now | 4, 5, 7, 10 |
| S3-2 | Content holds no numbers: hulls and fittings name their Book 10 row (`balanceRow`), and content lint checks the row exists | Book 10 and balance.json are the only source of numbers; Book 10 section 01 says content refers to keys | Copy stats into content | 2 (section 04), 10 (section 01) |
| S3-3 | `FIT_GRAPNELS_BOARDING_PLANKS` uses Book 10's row "Grapnels and planks" (Book 7 says "Grapnels and boarding planks"). Fitting slots include Book 7's gunwale, bow identity and mast identity. How those count against slot totals is open: Book 7 gives T3 hulls six slots, but Book 10 gives the knarr 5 | The books name and count them differently; Book 10 wins on numbers, so the slot rules wait for Stage 11 | Decide slot counting now | 7 (section 03), 10 (section 05) |
| S3-4 | Seeded ties are only the existing relationships Book 5 states: Gudrun and Arnbjörn, Gudrun and Ylva, Kári and Kolla (kin); Tófi and Ylva, Kolbeinn and Asa (spouses); Runa and Asa (sisters). Ties must be reciprocal | No romance system; residents may arrive with existing relationships (Book 1 section 02, Book 2 section 06) | Infer more ties from prose | 5 (sections 02, 08) |
| S3-5 | `Person.origin` is `UNSTATED` and each person's values come only from Book 5's text (Nessa, Ylva and Asa have none stated). The starting crew begin `missing` and are found in Chapter 1 | Book 5 does not give every origin; inventing them would be a design change. Stage 8 (people) settles origins | Infer origins from names | 2 (section 06), 5 |
| S3-6 | Saves are JSON with a header and a deflated (fflate), base64-encoded world state. Slots: `manual-1` to `manual-9`, three rotating autosaves chosen by a save sequence number (not the wall clock), and `checkpoint:<label>`. The browser store is localStorage, with every access guarded | Book 2 section 20 asks for compressed JSON with a header and hash; the sequence keeps rotation deterministic | IndexedDB (later if saves outgrow localStorage) | 2 (section 20) |
| S3-7 | The design set's revision in balance.json is read from Book 1 ("Revision 2.1"), because Book 10's markdown does not state it | balance.json already carried revision 2.1; the generator must reproduce the file exactly | Hard-code it | 10 (section 01) |
| S3-8 | WorldState follows Book 2 section 05 field for field, except that weather is absent until Stage 13 and tide is computed (S2-5); `Deed.doer` is an ID or "HOUSEHOLD" | The sea map owns weather; storing derived tide invites drift | Placeholder weather | 2 (section 05) |
| S3-9 | Content lint runs as part of `npm run lint`, and also alone as `npm run content:lint`. A list with a bad item still checks its good items, so one run reports every problem | CI then enforces content correctness on every push | Separate CI step | 2 (section 04) |

## 2026-11-10, Stage 4 (WebGL renderer and art pipeline)
Approved by: pending the user's review (the user authorised Stages 1 to 6 to continue without waiting).

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S4-1 | People and props are authored as material maps (one letter per material, plus marks such as eyes). A committed, deterministic generator (`src/tools/art/materials.ts`) turns each map into the palette-coded sheet: ink outline, three-tone shading lit from the upper left, the idle breath frames, and the unarmed west facing mirrored from east before shading. The palette-coded sheet is committed and is what the compiler reads; art lint fails if it falls out of step with its map | Book 7 section 11 allows deterministic generator scripts committed with their output. Shading after mirroring keeps the light from the upper left | Hand-shade every frame | 7 (section 11) |
| S4-2 | Sheet names add `prop_` (scenery such as the Grey Men stones) and `decal_` (blood decals) to Book 7's `actor_`, `ship_`, `token_`, `tile_`, `bld_` and `fx_` | Book 7's table has no pattern for scenery props or gore decals, which Book 2 section 19 keeps as separate sheets | Put them under `fx_` | 7 (section 11) |
| S4-3 | One atlas per sheet (PNG plus JSON manifest) in `assets/atlases/` | Few sheets so far; shared atlases can come when the sheet count makes draw calls matter | Shared atlases now | 2 (section 19) |
| S4-4 | Lighting and cutaway art direction lives in `assets/art/render.json`: ambient light per phase (day 255,255,255; dusk 240,196,160; night 92,116,164), light pools in 3 bands, and a 50% cutaway fade | These are art direction from Book 7 section 08, not tuning numbers, and Book 10 has none for them | Put them in balance.json | 7 (section 08), 10 |
| S4-5 | A tall object drawn in front of the player and overlapping them is cut away with a 50% ordered-dither fade (the 4 x 4 Bayer pattern) | Book 7 section 01 asks for "a cutaway or fade"; a dithered fade keeps the pixel look and costs no extra art | Cut-away sprites per object | 7 (section 01) |
| S4-6 | Renderer check views stay in the published build at `?view=art-test&case=front|behind|dusk|night` and `?view=sheet&sheet=<name>` | The browser tests need them, and the user can see the renderer and the art at the published link | Test-only build | 3 (Stage 4) |
| S4-7 | The steerer's body model A wears a madder-red tunic, a weld-green cloak pinned at the shoulder, woad trousers and undyed leg-wraps. Skin uses undyed wool, bleached linen and shingle, because the Book 7 palette has no skin ramp | Book 7 section 07 describes Norse household dress; the palette's cloth colours make a restrained, readable figure | Add skin tones to the palette (a Book 7 change for the user) | 7 (sections 07, 08) |
| S4-8 | The idle animation is 4 frames at 15 ticks each | Book 7 sets idle at 4 frames but gives no timing, and Book 10 lists none for idle | Another timing | 7 (section 01) |

## 2026-11-10, Stage 5 (input, interface shell and audio engine)
Approved by: pending the user's review (the user authorised Stages 1 to 6 to continue without waiting).

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S5-1 | Default controls (all remappable). Keyboard and mouse: WASD or arrows move, Shift run, C sneak, E interact, left mouse light attack, right mouse heavy, Q parry, left Ctrl block, Space dodge, R shove, V finisher, Tab command ring, 1 to 6 orders, M map, I inventory, B build, J report, Esc system menu. Gamepad: the standard layout, with quick orders 1 to 3 on the d-pad and 4 to 6 on the keyboard only | No book names keys; this follows common action-game layouts | Another layout | 2 (section 21) |
| S5-2 | Hold-or-toggle covers run, sneak, block and the command ring. Charged heavy and the finisher stay held, because holding is their mechanic | Book 2 section 21 asks for hold-or-toggle inputs without listing them | Toggle every hold | 2 (sections 12, 21) |
| S5-3 | A key or button pressed and released between two frames still counts as one press; a key let go and pressed again between two frames counts as a new press; key auto-repeat does not. The system key is handled before other menu keys and closes whatever is open, so "close, then open the map" works when both land in one frame | The browser tests found taps being lost (and, on the slower CI machine, Esc then M reopening the system menu); at low frame rates players tap that fast | None | 2 (section 21) |
| S5-4 | The safe rule reads a count of hostiles aware of the player (`ThreatView`), which the AI supplies from Stage 9. If a hostile becomes aware while a menu is open, the menu closes; the system menu stays as an overlay and time resumes | Book 2 section 02; closing keeps danger from ever being paused away | Leave the menu open | 2 (section 02) |
| S5-5 | Interface text is HTML on an overlay over the frame, sized by the 100 to 200% text-size option; the pixel-art fonts stay for art such as the title | Book 7 section 01 allows UI text on an accessible overlay at native resolution; screen readers can read it | Pixel fonts for all text | 2 (section 21), 7 (section 01) |
| S5-6 | All 16 audio cues are temporary synthesized tones, flagged `temporary: true` in `content/audio.json`. Their tempos and bar lengths are provisional and will come from the composed music | Book 11 section 11 plans "temporary cues clearly flagged" at Stage 5 and none by Stage 30; the composer is still open | Silence | 11 (sections 03, 11) |
| S5-7 | Music states have captions too (for example "Music: drums, the alarm is raised"); ambience has none | The alarm drums carry gameplay meaning, and Book 11 section 09 asks for captions on every gameplay-relevant sound | Captions for sound effects only | 11 (section 09) |
| S5-8 | The audio table maps named game events (HORN_BLOWN, PARRY, HEAVY_CHARGE_LEVEL_2, SIGN_RAN, DISCOVERY, ALARM_HORN) to cues ahead of the systems that will emit them | Book 11 section 10: sim events map to audio IDs through a content table | Wire audio straight into each system | 11 (section 10) |
| S5-9 | A Stage 5 check view is published at `?view=shell`: controls, menus with a test hostile, the order ring, captions and temporary sound | It lets the user try the shell before Stage 6 puts it in the game | Test-only | 3 (Stage 5) |

## 2026-11-10, Stage 6 (player controller and the opening)
Approved by: pending the user's review (the user authorised Stages 1 to 6 to continue without waiting). S6-4 holds numbers Book 10 does not give: please confirm or replace them.

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S6-1 | Wreck Strand's tile plan (96 x 64 tiles: Book 9 blocks 12 to 23 by 23 to 30) is expanded from the island grid by a committed, deterministic generator (`src/tools/levels/zones.ts`, plan in `content/zones/zone_sk_strand.plan.json`). Each tile reads the block under it through a smooth warp of up to 3 tiles, so coasts wander instead of following block lines; rock blocks break into skerries; the Kelda stream becomes a 2-tile channel running on over the shore. Content lint fails if the generated map falls out of step | Book 9 draws Skarvholm only at block scale (one character per 8 x 8 tiles); Book 9 section 03 asks for zones of about 96 x 64 tiles | Hand-draw 6,000 tiles | 9 (sections 01 to 05) |
| S6-2 | The high-tide line: shingle within about 3 tiles of the flats or the sea is foreshore, below the tide line; it is drawn darker and wet, and dry shingle meets it with a line of weed and wrack. A carried person may be set down only on dry shingle or machair; the foreshore, the stream and the flats refuse with the reason "The tide comes this far" | Book 4 asks Hild to be carried "above the tide line", and Book 9's shingle class covers both sides of that line | Only the flats count as below the line (then Hild would already be safe) | 4 (section 08), 9 (section 03) |
| S6-3 | The world state is now version 2: `onFoot` holds the zone, the steerer's position, facing, gait and wanted movement, the people in the zone with their pose (down, idle, sit, carried), and who is being carried. The v1-to-v2 migration adds `onFoot: null`; a v1 save has no positions, so its steerer starts at the zone spawn. Golden save 1 is kept frozen at schema 1 and must load through the migration; golden save 2 (the scripted rescue, then two days) is new | Book 2 section 05's world state has no on-foot positions, which a save must hold | Keep positions outside the saved state | 2 (sections 05, 20) |
| S6-4 | `content/onfoot.json`: interaction radius 1.5 tiles (the nearest target is marked), a collision footprint of 10 x 4 pixels shared by every person, and a carried person set down 14 pixels ahead of the steerer. Provisional: **these need the user's approval** | Book 2 section 21 asks for a "generous interaction radius" and Book 7 for one shared footprint, but Book 10 gives no numbers for them. Speeds come from Book 10 (sneak 1.75, walk 3.5, run 6.0, carry 1.8 tiles per second) | Other values | 2 (section 21), 7 (section 07), 10 |
| S6-5 | `content/opening.json`: Hild first calls 1 second after waking, then every 6 seconds until found, cycling three lines; if the steerer has not moved after 8 seconds, a single hint names the movement keys. That hint is the only control text in the opening | Book 4: "control in seconds"; Book 3 check 2: teach without a control list. The timings are scenario writing, not Book 10 tuning | No hint at all | 3 (Stage 6), 4 (section 08) |
| S6-6 | Hild's opening lines, written to her Book 8 voice and tagged ADDRESS_STEERER: "Here! Over here, past the rocks!", "Is that you? I'm by the rocks. The water's coming in!", "Over here! I'd come to you, but my leg has other ideas.", on being found "{steerer}! The sea spat you out too. Get me up the beach before it changes its mind.", at the tide line "Not here. The tide comes this far. Higher, up past the weed.", and once safe "That'll do. The sea's not having either of us today." | Book 8 gives Hild's voice and examples, not these lines | Other wording | 8 (sections 03, 06) |
| S6-7 | The strand ground is one generated sheet, `tile_strand` (`src/tools/art/terrain.ts`, recipe `assets/art/terrain/tile_strand.json`): sea, flooded flats, dry flats, foreshore, shingle, machair, rock and stream, six variants each, the water swaying over 4 frames; and edge tiles, three variants per direction, where a higher terrain laps over a lower one (foam at water, wrack at the tide line). Edge tiles are overlays, so art lint no longer requires them to be opaque | Book 7 section 11 allows generator scripts for tiles and variations; edges keep 24-pixel tiles from reading as a grid | Hand-drawn transition tiles for every pair | 7 (sections 11, 13) |
| S6-8 | A sheet may mark states as `pose.<state>: lying, carried or seated`. Art lint holds standing poses to the Book 7 adult size (about 18 x 30 in a 32 x 40 canvas) and lying and seated ones to the canvas only; a carried person is off the ground, so the feet-on-pivot check does not apply | Book 7's size is for a standing adult; Hild lying down cannot be 30 pixels tall | Larger canvases for these poses | 7 (sections 01, 11) |
| S6-9 | Sheet names add `ui_` for marks drawn in the world (the interaction marker, a small ochre chevron over the nearest target) | Book 7's naming table has no pattern for them | `fx_` | 7 (section 11) |
| S6-10 | Body model B: the same dress, animations and collision footprint as model A, on a slighter frame (narrower shoulders and hips) with the hair grown long and tied back. Both are shown side by side with no label; screen readers hear "First body" and "Second body" | Book 7 section 07 asks for two unlabelled models sharing everything; it does not describe them | Other differences | 5 (steerer), 7 (section 07) |
| S6-11 | The name field may be left empty: the steerer is then named "Steerer", which Book 8 already allows as a form of address. Names are at most 24 characters | Waking needs no typing, so control comes within seconds | Require a name | 5, 8 (section 03) |
| S6-12 | Hild's look: a weld-green shawl (her cloak lost to the sea), a woad tunic, brown trousers with wrapped legs, a blond braid, her left leg bound in linen with a little blood | Book 5 gives Hild's character but not her appearance; Book 7 section 07 gives Norse household dress | Other colours | 5, 7 (section 07) |
| S6-13 | Lying, carried and seated poses reuse facings: Hild down faces east or west (south and north reuse them), carried north is carried south mirrored, sit west is sit east mirrored. Standing and walking poses have four authored facings (west mirrored from east, unarmed only, as S4-1) | A body lying or slung across shoulders has no strong facing; Book 7 asks four facings for people | Author every facing of every pose | 7 (section 01) |
| S6-14 | The opening's light: the hour either side of first light and of dark is drawn under the dusk ambience, so day 1 at 06:00 opens in dawn light | Book 10 gives light and dark times but no twilight; Book 7 section 08 gives the phases | Day light at once | 7 (section 08), 10 |

## 2026-11-10, Art-direction pass (a change request between Stages 6 and 7)
Approved by: the user approved the Step 1 mockups (`docs/art/reference/`) with one note on the walk, recorded as AP-1. The remaining entries follow from that approval and from the user's request to make the standard permanent.

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AP-1 | **Playtest note (the user, on the Step 1 walk):** "Movement seems too rapid and floaty. This looks more like running movement speed. Animation looks kinda like walking but isn't great." Walk speed drops from 3.5 to 2.0 tiles per second (Book 10). The walk cycle grows from 6 to 8 frames (Book 7): contact, down, passing and up for each foot, with arm swing and a body bob, 4 ticks a frame. Its 12-pixel stride moves the planted foot at 45 pixels per second against the body's 48, so the feet stay put instead of gliding. Book 9's walking route times are recalculated (main loop 192 s, cliff path 92 s, Grey Men to Seal Cave 61 s, Naust to the causeway 20 s, Carrick open approach 5 s). Run, sneak and carry speeds are unchanged; their cycles are retimed to their speeds the same way | At 3.5 tiles (about 4.9 m) per second, a figure 30 pixels tall must take steps far longer than its legs, so its feet slide: it reads as running and floating. Walking speed is about 0.8 body-heights a second; 2.0 tiles per second is still twice that, as games need | 2.5 tiles per second (still about 4.7 steps a second); keep 3.5 and only redraw | 7 (section 01), 9 (sections 02, 07), 10 (section 02) |
| AP-2 | **Palette:** `assets/palette/book07.json` grows from 28 colours to 254: the 28 originals plus 226 colours in 37 named, hue-shifted ramps (5 to 11 steps; shadows cooler, highlights warmer). Map sources name a ramp and step (`material.X: <ramp> <sh> <base> <hi>`, `mark.X: <ramp> <step>`); ink outlines use the ink ramp | The approved mockups were painted with these ramps; three flat tones per material could not give wet sand, foam, lichen or hue-shifted shadow | Keep 28 colours and dither (rejected in Step 1: muddy); free RGB (breaks palette discipline) | 7 (sections 08, 11, 14) |
| AP-3 | **Dual-grid autotiling** replaces Stage 6's edge overlay tiles (S6-7): each visual tile sits half a tile up-left, its corners are four world tiles, and each pixel takes the highest terrain whose blended coverage plus world-periodic noise passes one half. The zone generator adds a `bank` terrain (`b`, blocks movement like rock) where the machair stands over the shingle, broken by the plan's `cuts` where paths climb; footpaths wind north from each cut. Book 9 section 05 is block scale and does not draw the bank: this is detail added by the zone generator | Organic boundaries with no tile edges, a bank with real height, and collision that still matches what is drawn | Hand-painted transition sets (47 per terrain pair); marching squares on the world grid (boundaries at tile edges) | 7 (section 14), 9 (section 05 expanded by the generator) |
| AP-4 | **New sheet kinds and names:** kind `plant` (states `<kind><variant>`, seven frames leaning -3 to +3 pixels); `plant_<place>`, `decal_<place>`, `fx_<place>` for a place's particles and prints, `actor_<animal>` for wildlife; pose `flying` (off the ground, like `carried`). Book 7 section 11's naming table lists them | The plants lean by frame so the renderer can bend them in the wind and part them round walkers without leaving the palette; per-place sheets keep one atlas per place | One sprite per plant with a vertex shear (smears pixels) | 7 (section 11) |

Wet sand dries for a few minutes after the water. Visual only | Waves, swash, foam, wet and drying sand and a moving tide cannot be tiles | Animated water tiles (Stage 6: a band that never moved with the tide) | 7 (section 14) |
| AP-6 | **Light moved to a post pass; the living-world effects are WebGL only.** The sprite pass writes unlit colour; a post pass applies ambient and pools (the same arithmetic as before), then cloud shadows, shafts, gust sheen, sand drift, grade, mist and flashes. The CPU reference renderer stays the exact reference for the indexed pass and the light (the Stage 4 parity tests still pass pixel for pixel); scenes with effects are checked in the browser by `tests/browser/standard.spec.ts` and by captures, not against the CPU | Re-implementing shaders on the CPU would double every effect and its bugs; parity is kept where it means something | Effects on the CPU too (slow, duplicated); no CPU reference at all (loses the Stage 4 checks) | 2 (section 19) |
| AP-7 | **First and last light:** the `dusk` ambient in `assets/art/render.json` goes from (240, 196, 160) to (228, 214, 204), a grey half-light a little warm; low sun adds its own warm grade with cool shadow lift | The opening is at 06:00 in spring, before light; the old orange ambient doubled with the new grade and turned the cool strand orange | Keep the ambient and drop the grade | 7 (sections 08, 14) |
| AP-8 | **Wildlife and particles are visual and deterministic:** gulls, cormorant, seal and crabs are placed by the zone plan (`life`) and move on the visual clock; prints, dust, splashes, ripples, spray, blown sand and midges live in `src/scenes/zoneVisuals.ts`, use their own hash noise (never `Math.random`) and are never saved | Life without touching the simulation or saves; captures repeat exactly | Simulated animals (a later stage's job if they need to matter) | 7 (section 14) |

The first in-game measure was 71.5%: the dry sand was still, so sand drift and a grass sheen were added (now 95 to 98%) | The user asked for thresholds set from the approved mockup; nine tenths of its frozen-cloud figure leaves room only for the solid things the mockup also leaves still | 70% (too lenient: the first build passed it with the beach dead still) | 7 (section 14) |

| AP-13 | **Captures:** `npm run capture -- <label>` runs the game in capture mode (`?capture`: the page advances only when told, one 40 ms frame at a time), so the 10-second clip is exact at 25 fps on any machine. The Step 1 mockups and clips move from `docs/art/` to `docs/art/reference/` | Real-time video recording drops frames and blurs pixel art on a slow machine | Playwright video (lossy, real time) | 7 (section 14) |
| AP-14 | **People's idles:** the steerer sheets gain `look` (4 frames) and `idlewind` (4 frames, cloak held, fluttering) beside `idle`; the scene plays a look round after 3 s still and braces in a gust above 0.55 | Book 7 section 01 lists idle 4; the standard asks for weight shifts and looking round | Longer single idle | 7 (section 01, 14) |
| AP-15 | **Parts of the standard with nothing to draw yet:** fire and lantern flicker, smoke, rain, lightning, fog banks, a flogging sail, sheep, bees and butterflies, and residents at tasks are in Book 7 section 14 but not on Wreck Strand in the game, because nothing there makes them yet (the fire is Stage 7, weather Stage 13, residents Stage 8, the wreck's sail and Kelda's life Stage 10). The renderer already takes light pools, a flash and mist; each arrives with the stage that brings its source, under that stage's standard check | Honest scope: no fire, weather or residents exist in Stage 6 | Fake a fire or rain on the strand (contradicts the opening) | 7 (section 14) |
| AP-16 | **Effect detail fits the machine:** every session starts at low detail and steps up (to lite, then full) only after 45 frames in a row come faster than 30 ms; it steps down again (and stays down) when frames keep coming slower than ten a second; a software renderer stays low. Lite has no kelp, surf zone, open-water ripples or light shafts and contact shadows only; low has depth colour and the foam edge, ambient light and grade, no shadow pass and under half the plants. When WebGL reports a software renderer (SwiftShader, llvmpipe, Apple Software Renderer and the like) the game starts at low instead. The full water and post shaders are compiled only when full or lite detail is first drawn; low detail and the plain art scenes use small shaders that compile quickly (compiling the full ones froze CI's Firefox for over 30 s). `?quality=full|lite|low` forces a level; the standard's motion test and the captures use full, and reduced motion at low still keeps 71.7% of the screen moving (threshold 50%). Captures run at full | CI's macOS Firefox renders WebGL in software: with the full shaders its frames took seconds, so control took 13 to 23 s (Stage 6 check 1 is under 5 s) and the simulation, which caps steps per frame, crawled. A player on a weak machine would see the same | Lower the standard everywhere; drop Firefox from CI; precompute effects into textures (still costly per pixel) | 2 (section 22), 7 (section 14) |
| AP-17 | **The user confirmed** the extra mockup-first steps on Stages 7, 10 and 16 (AP-12): "Keep the extra mock-up." | The user's answer | Drop them | 3 |
| AP-18 | **The play renderer is prepared behind the title.** Once the title is painted and the atlases are loaded, the game builds the play renderer and draws the opening frame once with each body, waits for it to finish, and presents the title again in the same task, so it is never seen. Waking reuses it (`data-startup` shows `"prepared":1`; the Stage 6 control test checks it). If preparing fails, waking builds its own renderer as before | CI's macOS Firefox spent 6.4 s in the first `render` call and 2 s more finishing it (shader compiles and texture uploads happen for real only on first draw), which put control at 10 to 11 s against Stage 6's five seconds of the naming screen. The title waits for a key anyway, so the work belongs there. Preparation runs one step per task (build, link water, link post, draw each body), so the title still answers keys between steps; its step costs are on the canvas as `data-prepare`, and waking stops any step left. The Stage 6 control test lets the title finish preparing before pressing Enter, as a player reading the title does, and still measures five seconds from the naming screen | Compile in the background with `KHR_parallel_shader_compile` (not offered everywhere, and texture uploads would stay); a lower five-second check (rejected: never weaken a check) | 2 (start-up), 3 (Stage 6 check 1 measured unchanged) |

## 2026-11-10, Audio direction change (a change request, not a stage)
Asked by: the user. Step 1 only (the sound test page); wiring audio into the game waits for the user's approval of it.

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AU-1 | **The game generates all of its own audio.** No recordings, no commissioned composer, no voice acting, no licensed or sampled music; every tune, sound and ambience is original, written by Claude and played by the game. Stage 0 decision 19 changes from "Voiced barks at most" to generated audio with voice blips | The user's decision | Commissioned score and recorded barks (Book 11's former recommendation) | 11 (sections 01, 02, 08 to 11, new 12), 2 (section 18), 3 (decision 19, Stages 5, 7, 8, 9 and 30, rules), 8 (section 08), CLAUDE.md |
| AU-2 | **The synthesiser** (`src/audio/synth/`): one short sample per instrument generated at load at 32 kHz (plucked lyre and harp by Karplus-Strong; flute, whistle, drone, choir-like pad and horn from harmonics with seamless loops; bells from inharmonic partials; frame drum, hand percussion and a gull call), pitched per note SNES-style; 8 music voices with stealing; one shared echo (delay, feedback, low-pass) set per piece; a 7 kHz low-pass on the music. The same code runs on an `OfflineAudioContext`, so tests and renders need no speaker | SNES-like limits keep the music of a piece; generation keeps everything original and the build small | Additive synthesis per voice (costly, less of a piece); full sample libraries (excluded by AU-1) | 11 (sections 02, 10) |
| AU-3 | **Music as note data** in `content/music/<piece>.json`: tempo, metre (beats and steps), mode, echo, layers (instrument, gain, pan, echo send, group, bar-by-bar text such as `D5:2 A4 ~G4:3`, with `~` a cut, `!` an accent, `[ ]` a chord and `-` a bar covered by a tie) and seasonal variants (tempo, mode, pitch-class map, layers, resting bars). Tests check every bar against its metre, every pitched note against its mode in every season, and that no piece needs more than 8 voices | Readable, reviewable, checkable composition | A binary tracker format; MIDI files | 11 (section 10) |
| AU-4 | **The first three pieces** (original): the hearth theme in D Dorian, 6/8 at 54, 16 bars (35.6 s a pass), layers drone, lyre, flute (Hild's tune), hummed voice, harp and heartbeat drum, which are the household's growth layers; Skarvholm in A Dorian, 3/4 at 92, flute over a wind pad and drone with harp, hand percussion and seabirds in summer, and in winter A Aeolian at 74 with the harp and percussion gone, a bell, a longer echo and the melody resting through bars 9 to 12; the raid loop in E Aeolian, 4/4 at 84, a drone, held-breath pad, lyre ostinato and heartbeat ("calm"), replaced on the next bar line by war drums, bones and a horn call ("alarm") | Book 11 sections 03 to 05 | — | 11 |
| AU-5 | **The sound test page** is a second page of the published build (`sound.html`), with every item, layer switches, seasons, the alarm, wind and tide sliders, the gore setting, the six sliders and mono, and captions. It also renders any item offline for the tests and for `npm run audio:render` | The user asked for it as Step 1 | A `?view=` inside the game page (would load the whole game) | 11 (section 10) |
| AU-6 | **Voice blips:** one blip a syllable (vowel groups, a silent final e dropped), coloured by the vowel's formants, shaped by punctuation; profiles for Hild, Bran, Runa and Oddi from Book 5; the steerer has none. The page's lines for Bran, Runa and Oddi are test lines (`soundtest.line.*`), not barks; their real barks come with Stages 8 and 10 | Book 8 section 03 and Book 5 | Voiced barks (excluded by AU-1) | 8 (section 08), 11 (section 08) |
| AU-7 | **The tells:** Rán's sea-light is a muffled, beating hum under the water with a shimmer that always falls; Manannán's is hooves on water cantering past inside a mist shimmer that never resolves (a cluster with a lowered second) and a far horn; the draugr is a dripping that comes closer each drop over a slow wet breath | Book 11 section 07 (hooves on water for Manannán; dripping that follows for the draugr); the user's names | — | 11 |
| AU-8 | **Listening back is by measurement.** Claude cannot hear, so every item was rendered offline and checked by its levels, a pitch-class profile (which confirmed D Dorian, A Dorian in summer, A Aeolian in winter) and spectrograms. Changes made from that: the music low-pass from 9 to 7 kHz and a softer lyre pluck (the music was bright for the SNES top, centroid 2.0 to 2.3 kHz); the strand ambience down about 5 dB (it was louder than the music); voice blips up about 9 dB (they were near -40 dBFS); the level-1 charge warning, Manannán and the draugr louder; the echo taken off the parry and guard break (the echo repeated the parry's click, which would sound like several clangs); Rán's hum filtered softer; the plucked rings shortened and the drum stopped with its note (the hearth needed 11 voices at its peak); the raid's pad joins the heartbeat as "calm" so the alarm stays within 8 voices, and the alarm layers louder (the alarm raised the level only 1.2 times) | The user asked for a listen against the brief before showing it; the user's own ears are the real test | — | 11 (section 12) |
| AU-9 | **Until Step 2, the game itself still plays the Stage 5 temporary tones** and the temporary footsteps (AP-10); the generated audio lives on the sound test page only | The user asked to stop after Step 1 and wait for approval | Wire it in now | — |

## 2026-11-11, Audio direction change, Step 2 (approved by the user: "I'm happy with the sound test page")

| AU-10 | **Footsteps soft and natural**: low and dull (spectral centroid under about 1.3 kHz, shingle and water under about 2.3 kHz), never a sharp click (every step rises over at least 2 ms; heel then a softer roll onto the toe), drawn afresh every step from a seeded stream so no two are alike, different by surface (a soft thud on grass, a crunch on shingle, a hush on sand, a duller squelch on wet sand, a splash and drops in water, a dull knock on rock), by gait (running louder and quicker, sneaking much quieter, carrying heavier), and at least 10 dB under a light hit, the quietest thing in the mix. The sound test page compares the surfaces and gaits; `tests/browser/sound.spec.ts` measures each | The user: "the footstep sound is too harsh"; the Stage 5 footstep was a temporary noise burst with an instant attack | | 11 (07, 09) |
| AU-11 | **Generated audio wired into the game for everything built**: the synthesiser replaces the Stage 5 test tones in the shell and in play. Music cues name their piece (`piece`, `layers` in `content/audio.json`) and keep its tempo and metre, so changes land on its bar lines; the horn call, the alarm and discovery stingers and the hearth's fire are generated (`src/audio/synth/cues.ts`); the strand ambience follows the game's wind at the steerer and the drawn tide; Hild calls and speaks in voice blips panned to where she is; footsteps follow the surface and gait; music ducks under voices and stingers. The island theme enters once Hild is safe (the opening itself has no music, Book 11). The instruments' samples are made in a background worker from the title screen, so turning sound on never stalls a frame (a shell browser test caught the stall). Only the combat music still stands in (the raid's alarm, flagged temporary until Stage 9 composes it). ## 2026-11-11, Firefox and WebKit block publishing again
Book 3's Stage 12 acceptance check: both browsers must pass and block. This ends BR-1 and S1-18's exception for WebKit.

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-2 | **The browser tests that play the game run stepped.** The controls tests and the opening rescue open play with `?capture` and advance it a frame of fixed length at a time (`window.oeStep`; helpers in `tests/browser/strand.ts`: `step`, `stepUntil`, `stepUntilText`, a stepped `walkAxis` that slows near its goal and never overshoots). Their timed checks are measured in game time and are unchanged: the slot name shown briefly (now also checked gone after 1.5 s), Hild's call within 5 s, each prompt within 5 s, the camera held still for 10 s after 3 s with no pixel stepped back and at most three positions; the rescue's "only the next thing to do" check now reads every frame instead of one sample each 100 ms. A frame is 100 ms (the most the visuals take in one frame, and neither fast nor slow for AP-16's detail check); walking frames are 250 ms (the most the simulation catches up). The tests print the page's errors. Control within five seconds of the naming screen stays a real-time check | CI's macOS Firefox renders WebGL in software at about four frames a second: the real-time walks overshot (the steerer ended 18 tiles past Hild), the camera was still settling after 13 s and a 1.5 s slot name was missed between polls. A stepped game plays the same frames on any machine. Firefox then passed four runs in a row (runs 43042975688, 43045765119, 43048735601, 43048858617), with the published-address check | Longer timeouts on wall-clock waits (still flaky); fewer checks (rejected: never weaken a check) | 3 (Stage 6 checks 1, 2, 5, 8, 9, measured unchanged) |
| BR-3 | **Captions and notices raised by simulation events expire on the frame's clock** (`src/scenes/playView.ts`), not the wall clock | They were stamped with `performance.now()` but expired against the frame's time, so stepped play (which runs ahead of the wall clock) dropped Hild's "Get me up the beach" at once. In normal play the two clocks agree within a frame, so players see no change | Leave it and wait in real time in the test | 2 (runtime) |
| BR-4 | **WebKit runs on Linux inside Playwright's own image** (`mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.63.0-noble`, pinned to the Playwright version in `package.json`), for the push and the published address; Firefox stays on `macos-15-intel`. This replaces S1-16 for WebKit | On the macOS runners WebKit's WebGL context is lost as soon as it is made, headless or headed (the probe reads renderer "null", lost: true), so no test that draws could pass. Ubuntu's package mirrors were unreachable from the runners, so WebKit's libraries come with the image. There it has WebGL2 and runs the whole suite in about a minute. Linux Firefox was also tried: headless has no WebGL2; headed under Xvfb works (llvmpipe) but its slower clicks broke the five-second check once, so Firefox stays where it passes | Keep WebKit on macOS (cannot pass); WebKit headed on macOS (same lost context); install WebKit's libraries from Ubuntu's mirrors (unreachable) | 3 (Stage 1 check 2), S1-9, S1-16 |
| BR-5 | **The art check views (`?view=art-test`, `?view=sheet`) draw their scene again every frame**, as play does (`src/scenes/artView.ts`) | They drew once. The canvas keeps no drawing buffer between frames, and WebKit's screenshots of a frame drawn once came out black (0% drawn) while play, drawn every frame, was fully drawn. Drawn every frame, the WebKit renderer matches the CPU reference pixel for pixel | `preserveDrawingBuffer` for those views (costs every frame in play too) | 2 (render) |
| BR-6 | **`tests/browser/webgl-probe.ts`** prints, in each Firefox and WebKit job's log, what WebGL2 the browser offers and (for WebKit) how much of the art view and of play it draws; failed jobs keep their screenshots and page snapshots as an artifact for 7 days. Playwright installs on Linux fall back to the browser alone when Ubuntu's mirrors are unreachable | So a runner's graphics can be judged from the log next time | | |
| BR-7 | **Publishing waits for Chromium (Linux), Firefox (macOS) and WebKit (Linux)**: the deploy needs all three jobs, and neither Firefox nor WebKit has `continue-on-error`, here or against the published address. **Ends BR-1 and S1-18** | Book 3 Stage 12 acceptance check; both now pass | | 3 (Stage 12), CLAUDE.md (its BR-1 sentence is now out of date: for the user to update) |

## 2026-11-11, Playtest notes: hands-on tasks and the camera

The user's playtest note, verbatim: "i dont like the timing mini game for menial tasks. driftwood should be found in a variety of spots and picked up without a mini game. perhaps could be retained for fishin. otherwise camera movement is a little jarring and needs to be smoothed. more noticeable when changing directions than with mouse. otherwise continue"

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PT-0 | **The camera springs its point in the world, not its offset from the steerer.** The keyboard's lead in the direction of travel eases round over Book 10's new `keyboardLeadHalfLife` (0.3 s) instead of flipping in a frame. The drawn view is whole pixels: it keeps its offset from the steerer's pixel while within the snap of where the camera wants it (a steady walk never jitters), and otherwise takes the camera's own pixel. A step against the camera's motion, or back the way it last stepped, waits while within a pixel and a half. On a turn the view now slows, pauses and comes round at most 2.5 times the walking speed; before, it reversed at once and flung round at six times the walking speed (3 px a frame). Tested in `tests/unit/camera.test.ts`; the cursor's look-ahead and the still-cursor check are unchanged | The user found the camera jarring, most of all when changing direction on the keyboard. The old spring moved the offset from the steerer, so the steerer's own reversal and the lead's swing added up in the same frame | A longer cameraHalfLife alone (slower everywhere, still jolts on a turn); a camera locked to the steerer (no look-ahead) | 7 (14), 10 (21) |
| PT-1 | **Menial hands-on tasks are a plain action; only fishing from the shore keeps the timed press.** Lighting the fire, cooking, tending and building play their work loop for Book 10's `actionSeconds` with no timing ring and no press, and add work at the steerer's skill. Book 10's new `timedTasks` key names the timed tasks (fishing from the shore only): there the ring closes over the water and a good press adds up to `playerTimingBonus`; missing it never ruins the catch. A press during a plain action does nothing; use, interact and dodge still behave as during any work (dodge stops it). The "as the mark closes" prompt and the `TIMING_GOOD` sound come only with the ring. **Replaces part of S7-8** | The user's playtest note: "i dont like the timing mini game for menial tasks ... perhaps could be retained for fishin" | Remove the timed press everywhere; keep it as an option | 2 (07), 3 (Stage 7), 7 (10), 10 (08, 09) |
| PT-2 | **Driftwood is found, not worked.** Pieces lie at 12 spots on Wreck Strand (`content/camp.json`, the DRIFTWOOD sites): along the high-tide line at both ends, under the bank at both ends, among the rocks of both outcrops, and by the wreck's stern. A plain interact picks one up: the steerer stoops for one gather loop (0.8 s, no timing ring), the piece's load goes into the camp's store at once, and its game minutes pass. The camp task `TASK_GATHER_DRIFTWOOD` is now this pick-up (its cost read from Book 10's "Driftwood finds"; the "Gather driftwood" row of "Homestead tasks" is gone); the five `wrack_*` sites are replaced by the pieces. The residents' DRIFTWOOD job is unchanged (S8-29), and it does not use up the pieces. The pieces can be picked up once the camp's work opens (S7-26), as the weed line's driftwood could; before that they are scenery | The user's note: "driftwood should be found in a variety of spots and picked up without a mini game". Book 9 already has driftwood along the high-tide line, coming in after storms | A pickup item in the hotbar (driftwood is a store, not a tool); one spot that refills | 2 (07), 9 (03), 10 (03, 09) |
| PT-3 | **Book 10 "Driftwood finds"** (section 03): 12 spots; a piece at every spot at the first dawn (the wreck's storm: 6 loads); each dawn 2 pieces wash up in spring and summer, 3 in autumn and 4 in winter, twice that after a storm (none until Stage 13's weather), never more than the spots picked bare; 0.5 loads a piece; a pick-up takes 5 game minutes and no stamina, with a 0.8 s stoop. **Why these numbers:** a dawn's pieces bring 1, 1, 1.5 and 2 loads, so the steerer alone keeps the camp fire (`houseFuelPerDay` 0.5, 0.2, 0.5 and 1.0) with some over, matching the old "1 load per search" in spring and summer and coming near Book 10's richer autumn and winter rates. `campFuel` stays 0 and `fireLight` 1 load, so the first fire still needs 2 pieces fetched up to the broch first. A piece is half a search's load so that fetching is a few walks, not one | The user left the numbers to us; they must fit the existing rates and fuel use | 1 load a piece (the strand would hold 12 days of spring fuel at once); 5 spots (not "a variety"); refill each piece on its own timer | 10 (03) |
| PT-4 | **Picked-up pieces are kept in the world state's region resource nodes** (`regions.REG_SK.resourceNodes`, Book 2 section 10's `resourceNodes`): a spot picked bare has an entry with the next day it can be refilled. At dawn (with the day's ration) the sea refills the season's number of bare spots, chosen by a hash of the campaign seed, the day and the spot (no random stream is drawn). The save shape does not change, so the world state stays at **version 7**: no migration and no new golden save; an older save loads with every piece lying there. Golden save 7 is regenerated, as its scenario now picks up driftwood | The shape Book 2 already gives regions for nodes that come back; another stage was changing version 7 in the same tree | A new `home.driftwood` field (version 8, a migration, golden save 8) | 2 (10, 20) |
| PT-5 | **The driftwood art** (`prop_driftwood`, `assets/art/authoring/driftwood.py`): a bleached forked branch, a short log with its root knot, and a broken spar with a trenail, each also drawn the other way round, from the palette's driftwood ramp with a contact shadow; a frond of wrack caught on each stirs in the wind (4 frames, out of step from piece to piece). Rendered at 1x and 4x with a GIF of a pick-up in `docs/art/playtest-driftwood/`. No glint (Book 7 section 10: common stuff, not an item). The three Stage 7 driftwood props at the old weed-line sites (S7-19) are taken out of the strand's plan, so no log there looks as if it could be picked up; the strand's other debris (the long logs, planks, barrel, net, rope) is the approved reference's and stays | Each spot is a visible piece of driftwood art | Reuse the debris logs (too big to carry; no shadow of their own) | 7 (10, 14), 9 (03) |
| PT-6 | **The pick-up's sound** `SFX_DRIFTWOOD_PICKUP` (generated, captioned "Driftwood lifted from the shingle"): a short scrape of wood on stones, a few pebbles trickling off it, and a hollow knock as it is tucked under the arm, played as the stoop begins. `SFX_WORK_DRIFTWOOD` (the old search) stays registered for the residents' job but is no longer played for the steerer | Book 11 section 12: every system adds its sounds, with captions | Reuse the search's sound (it is a longer armful of knocks) | 11 (07) |
| PT-7 | **Words:** the prompt names the pick-up ("E  Pick up the driftwood"), the fire's hint says to pick it up on the strand, the morning report says how many pieces the tide left, and a plain action shows no "as the mark closes" prompt. The fishing tip keeps its press | The tutorial hints spoke of gathering | | 8 |

Built under the standing authority of DD-11: nothing waited on the user's approval; every choice is logged here and the ones that most want a look are flagged in the stage report.

The Kelda Spring is a pool carved in the machair zone, north-east of the broch, with its outflow to the burn, not a zone of its own | Book 9's tile plans place Kelda beside the steading; the cairn and the Hag's Seat lie south-east and south-west of it on the block grid | Kelda as its own zone | 4 (02), 9 (02 to 05) |
| S10-2 | **Several links on one edge:** a zone may link more than one zone across the same edge; the crossing picks the link whose neighbour lies beyond the steerer's point along that edge (`linkOf`); the machair links south to the strand, west to the Hag's Seat and east to the cairn | The southern zones sit side by side under the machair's south and west edges | One link per edge, with corridor zones | 2 (09) |

## 2026-11-12, Stage 11 (boat repairs and the first fitting)

Built under the standing authority of DD-11: nothing waited on the user's approval. The Seal Cave mockup was drawn before the place was built (S11-17); every choice the books leave open is logged here, and the ones that most want a look are flagged in the stage report.

| ID | Decision | Why | Alternatives | Books |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S11-1 | **Which boat is repaired.** The household's T1 coastal boat is an old six-oared boat drawn up under the bank on Wreck Strand (its near keel at tile (42, 27); tiles (39 to 45, 25 to 26) solid), holed, unrigged and without its steering oar. The approved earlier drawing on the skerry stays as the wreck of Thorkel's own boat (its stern is the piece washed up at the east end), and the rigging is salvaged from it. Hild's Book 8 line "Father would have hated this boat. That's how I know it's sound." is said when the three repairs are done | Book 1 has "a surviving small coastal boat repaired on the beach, not a longship built from debris"; Book 4 and Book 9 have the rigging taken from "the wreck section on the skerry"; the stern is "the stern of Thorkel's boat, broken off in the wreck", so that boat cannot be patched | Thorkel's boat refloated from the skerry (its stern is gone); a boat built from debris (Book 1 forbids) | 7 (05), 9 (03) |
| S11-2 | **The ship model** (`src/sim/ship/ship.ts`): Book 2's `ShipInstance` as `ships.flagship` (Book 2 has no ship prefix, so the key is not a content ID), with where it lies (`lies`), Chapter 1's repairs (`repairs`, each LACKING, MATERIALS with its work in worker-minutes, or DONE, and how it was come by) and a fitting under way; `fittings` keyed by the hull's slot labels, from a slot layout per hull in content/hulls.json whose length the test holds to Book 10's Slots (T3b has 5 there, though Book 7 says T3 hulls have six) | Book 2 section 08 gives the shape without the Chapter 1 repairs | Repairs as facts only | 2 (08) |
| S11-3 | **`deriveStats`** reads each fitting's effect and drawback from Book 10's own cells, term by term ("Cargo +2 (+1 on T1)", "Oar -1; rowing crew +1"), and fails on a term it does not know; refits read the same way; wear at Book 10's wearLeak takes an oar step; fewer rowers than the rowing crew slow the oars in proportion (Book 7 section 02); a ship lacking a repair is not seaworthy, and without rigging has no sail; the wrecked hull's integrity is Book 10's, the patched hull's too | Book 10 is the only source of numbers | A second, machine table of modifiers in Book 10 (two copies of each number) | 2 (08), 10 (05) |
| S11-4 | **Book 10 "Chapter 1 repairs"** (section 05): wreckedIntegrity 30, patchedIntegrity 80 (made good with iron after Carrick, Book 6), rigging 1 worker-day, shipOar 0.125, carveOar 2, fitHold 1, fitBowPlatform 1, oarFood 3 rd; the hull patch's materials and labour are the existing Carpentry row "Hull patch (Chapter 1)" (4 boards, 30 rivets, 2 worker-days), salvaged together as one load | Book 4 gives the repairs without numbers; Chapter 1 has no iron for Book 10's self-built fittings | Book 10's self-built fitting costs (they need iron) | 10 (05) |
| S11-5 | **Every material is one load** (Book 3 check 3, the user's no-grind direction): the boards and rivets lie on the shingle just east of the boat (49.4, 26.6), the rigging is cut free of the wreck at low water and carried back over the flats, the oar comes from Seal Cave; set down within 4 tiles of the boat (content, `layByTiles`) a load is laid by it and drawn there. The boards were first placed in the debris at the strand's east end; carried along the foreshore at high water the load was set down whenever the bar ran out, a chore with no choice in it, so they now lie near the boat | Book 4: "patch the hull with salvaged boards"; the repair's weight is its labour, not the fetching | Four boards at four spots; the boards in the east debris | 9 (03), 10 (05) |
| S11-6 | **One fitting at a time, from salvage**: the extended hold or the raised bow platform, chosen when looking the boat over, made by the work at the boat, and taken off again in one action (its labour is spent again to fit it again). On T1 the shield rack takes no slot (Book 7 gives it the gunwale, "free on T2+"); the rig slot is T2's | Book 4: "one reversible customisation: more cargo space, or a raised fighting position at the bow" | Free swaps; a refund of labour | 4 (08), 7 (03) |
| S11-7 | **Cutting the rigging free and taking a fitting off are each one "Repair the ship" action** (Book 10 section 09: 30 game minutes, 2 stamina, a plain action with no timing, PT-1); the steerer's own work at the boat is the same action, adding its minutes at the steerer's shipRepair skill | One row for every hands-on ship task | Rows of their own (first written, then removed as duplicates) | 10 (05, 09) |
| S11-8 | **The shipwright's job** (`SHIPWRIGHT`, Book 1's "a shipwright maintains the ship"): open only while there is work at the boat (a material laid by it, a fitting under way, or the carver's oar); Runa takes it up herself once her own job has no work (`then` in content/household.json), and anyone can be given it through "Work"; work goes to the hull, then the rigging, then the oar, then the fitting; only Runa carves (carpentry 2 or more) | "Crew work continuing while the player explores", Book 3 Stage 11 | A shipwright fixed by role (Stage 23's household screen) | 1 (10), 2 (06) |
| S11-9 | **The steering oar's four ways**, recorded as `repair.oar`: **TRADE** at the scavengers' parley (Thorkel's silver if the chest was saved and not given, or Book 10's oarFood of salt fish); **THEFT** taken from the skiff unseen (a THEFT deed with the living scavengers as victims and no witness); a theft **seen** is a THEFT with witnesses and the fight starts; **FIGHT** once the fight is on, the oar is the fight's whoever still stands; **FOUND** if all three are dead; **CARVED** by Runa over two worker-days, asked for at the boat. The scenario tests play each | Book 4: "fight, steal, trade, or have Runa carve a new one over two days" | Theft only by night (it is easier then, not required) | 4 (08), 6 (01) |
| S11-10 | **Where the scavengers are**: after the sheepfold by any road (fought, sneaked past or paid) the living go to their skiff: Seal Cave's encounter (`SCN_SK_SEALCAVE`) places only those not dead or captive, with the fold's parley; before the fold, the skiff is unguarded. Stage 10's code that took them off the map when the steerer left the fold now takes them off only at the fold. A careful player beats the one who ran from the fold in 2 of 4 seeds or more (test floor); three at once alone is hard, and the trade or the theft are there | Book 10's threat schedule has them return to Seal Cave; three on one without Bran is the fold's fight without its ally | Respawn all three | 3 (Stage 11), 4 (08) |

| S11-12 | **Facts and beats**: `ch1.boat_ready` and BEAT_1_REPAIRS when all three repairs are done; BEAT_1_CUSTOMISE at the first fitting; each repair and fitting goes into the ship's history log (Book 7 section 02) | | | 2 (08, 16) |
| S11-13 | **Thorkel's rites are asked for at the bier**: once the body lies by the broch and Hild is within 10 tiles of it with the steerer, she raises it once, and her words there offer "a ship of stones on the machair" or "the sea, with his gold ring for Rán" (or not yet). For the ship-setting the household lays the stones for 2 game hours on the machair (22, 35) and Bran carries the body there; for the sea burial they gather on the strand (33, 35.6). The rite begins when the steerer comes within 10 tiles: the lament, a line each, 60 game minutes, then the body is laid to rest | Book 4: "optional, recommended by Hild" | A scripted cutscene; rites asked for at the fire | 4 (08), 8 |

No deed is written: Book 6's RITES_DENIED is for rites never given, decided at the launch (Stage 12) | Like the searches' reactions: the household's own feeling, not a deed weighed by witnesses | A RITES deed tag (Book 6 has none) | 6 (01), 10 (08) |

## 2026-11-12, Stage 12 (the launch and the Build 1 gate)

Built under the standing authority of DD-11: nothing waited on the user's approval; every choice the books leave open is logged here, and those that most want a look are flagged in the stage report. The build gate stops here for the user's playtest.

| ID | Decision | Why | Alternatives | Books |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S12-1 | **The launch is Book 2's first story gate**: its state is a `GateState` in the world's `gates` (`BEAT_1_LAUNCH`: CLOSED, OPEN, STARTED, PASSED; content/launch.json is its `GateDef`, kind VOYAGE); its scene's state is kept in facts (`launch.*`) and the ship (`lies` moves with it) | Book 2 section 16 gives the gate's shape; the scene is short and one-off | A scene state object of its own | 2 (05, 16) |
| S12-2 | **The gate opens once the boat is seaworthy and the three searches are decided** (each search's fact set, whatever came of it): Chapter 1's goals are met before its point of no return. Until then, looking the boat over says "Not everyone is found yet" | Book 4's Chapter 1 goal is "rescue four crew, make the boat seaworthy"; the oar can be stolen before Bran's search, so the boat alone could be ready with people still out there | Open on the boat alone (a player could sail with Bran unfound) | 4 (08), 6 (15) |
| S12-3 | **Book 10 "The launch"** (section 22): floats Knee; skids 8 tiles; slideSpeed 1.8; rowSpeed 1.2; rowOut 9 tiles; trialRow 60 game minutes; tideLookahead 24 game hours; storesShort 3 days. Section 22's two tables are given headings ("Gates", "Gate rules") so `npm run balance` keeps both; no value changed (as S8-2) | No number may live in code | | 10 (22) |
| S12-4 | **The launch waits for the tide.** The boat floats off only where the water at its keel, stern to stem, is knee deep within its 8 tiles of skids; the readiness screen shows the tide's line and, when it is out, about when the water will float the boat; the start is refused while it is out (`GATE_REFUSED`, TIDE_OUT). On the strand the boat floats about 70% of the day | Book 7 section 14: what is drawn never disagrees with the simulation; a boat launched onto dry flats would. A learnable rule like the rigging's low water | Launch at any tide over the flats (the boat would slide 20 tiles at low water) | 7 (14), 10 (22) |
| S12-5 | **The readiness screen's lines**: the boat (its repairs and fitting); the tide; the crew (the rowers against the hull's rowing crew from `deriveStats`, so the extended hold's extra oar shows as short); food in store in days; the weapons in hand and arrows; allies ("Not known": no one owes the household anything yet); Thorkel's rites. Only the boat and the tide hold the start; the rest inform. Book 2's `ReadinessView` lines also carry their words' values and the IDs they list | Book 6 section 15: "the boat's repairs, crew and stores"; Book 2: crew, ship, stores, allies, equipment | Hold the start on short stores | 2 (16), 6 (15) |
| S12-6 | **Thorkel's rites close at the launch**: once the gate starts they can no longer be asked for; if they were never given, Book 6's `RITES_DENIED` is written then (the household deed, its crew as witnesses, friendship by Book 10's deed table), `ch1.rites` is DENIED and Hild says "We sail, and Father lies there with no rites." The readiness screen warns of it | S11-14 left RITES_DENIED "decided at the launch (Stage 12)"; it makes the point of no return mean something | Rites after the launch | 6 (01), 10 (08) |
| S12-7 | **Confirm, name and mark in one sitting**: the readiness screen, the name and the mark are planning screens (time stopped, Book 2 section 02); the gate's commands are sent together when the mark is chosen, so "Not yet" backs out until then; the labelled checkpoint `launch` is made as the gate starts. A gate started with no name or mark (a checkpoint, a hook) asks for them again, and the name and mark cannot be backed out of | Book 2 section 16: the readiness screen's confirmation makes the checkpoint and hands off; one place to change one's mind | Send the start at the readiness screen (a reload mid-name would then strand the player in the naming) | 2 (16, 20) |
| S12-8 | **The launch scene** (content/launch.json, its seconds the scene's choreography as S6-5's): the household and the steerer set hands to the boat from behind (a fade), heave 2.4 s, run it down its skids at Book 10's slideSpeed to its float line, climb aboard one by one (the steerer last), hoist the yard (2.2 s, the sail dropping with its mark) and row out east 9 tiles at Book 10's rowSpeed, a stroke a second; then the sea map. While it plays the steerer's own commands are not taken (only the launch's, the options and the telemetry), nothing is offered to do, and the boat is never cut away over the steerer | Book 7 section 05: "launching shows the crew pushing and climbing aboard"; real time, no pause (it plays in game time) | A cutscene out of the game's time | 7 (05) |

A new campaign creates the launch's gate CLOSED | Book 2 section 20 | | 2 (05, 20) |

Every coast is shown (Book 4: "the sea map reveals the coast"), but only Skarvholm is named and Carrick only as "Smoke on the Irish shore" (Book 7 section 10: unknown places as coastline only); no routes, wind or tide marks yet (Stage 13).
