## 2026-11-12, Design addendum 2.4 (approved by the user)
Approved by: the user, after a playtest of the opening ("build all of it", items marked proposal included). Addendum 2.4 wins over the books and over addenda 2.2 and 2.3 where they conflict; it is now folded into Books 1 to 4 and 6 to 11, and the books are the source of truth again. `docs/design-addendum-2.4.md` is kept as a record only. It is built as a change request after the Build 1 gate and before Stage 13 (DE-33). No game code changed in the fold; `npm run balance` regenerated `content/balance.json` (revision 2.5).

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DE-1 | **Combat v2: weighty, deliberate, Souls-like.** Few hits, and every hit matters; fights are won by reading the enemy, distance and stamina, and punishing mistakes, and are short and decisive. Book 1 section 09 is rewritten round it; Book 2 sections 12 and 13 gain the combat v2 rules, states (`lunge, parried, backstab, flinch, knockdown, getUp, sidestep, heal`) and data; Book 10 sections 06 and 07 its numbers. **Supersedes** Stage 9's combat rules where they differ: S9-2 (hitStagger, by poise), S9-3 and S9-5 kept, S9-7 extended by self-preservation, S9-29 kept as the base of "one or two press the attack" | Addendum section 1 and section 7's first entry; the playtest found fights long trades of damage | Tune combat v1's numbers only (would not give spacing, poise or commitment) | 1 (09, 15, 17, 18), 2 (05, 12, 13, 21, 23, 24), 3 (rules, Phase B, Stage 9), 7 (01, 07, 10, 12, 14), 10 (06, 07), 11 (07, 08, 12) |
| DE-2 | **The combat stance.** A melee weapon, shield or bow in hand puts the steerer in the stance, facing the cursor (the right stick; in keyboard-only play the way last moved) with a continuous facing that turns at 720 degrees a second; within 70 degrees of the facing movement is at walking speed, 70 to 110 degrees strafes at 0.85, beyond 110 backs off at 0.7 (the addendum's proposal); running turns the body to the way of travel and exposes the back. The body is drawn in the nearest of the four authored facings, while strike arcs, shields and backs use the true facing. Attacks commit: up to 30 degrees of turn in a windup, none from the active ticks to the end of recovery; only a light attack's first 4 ticks cancel into a dodge; a swing that meets no one adds 6 ticks of recovery. **Supersedes** DA-15's "melee towards the cursor snapped to the nearest facing" and Book 10's turnLock, once built | Addendum section 1, "Facing, lock-on and backing away"; four facings in the simulation cannot face a cursor or back away from a foe off the axes | Eight drawn directions (new art for every person and layer); keep four-way facing (the addendum asks for facing the cursor) | 1 (09, 17), 2 (12, 21), 7 (01, 10), 10 (02, 06, 21) |
| DE-3 | **Lock-on and its keys.** Lock-on takes the nearest enemy in front within 8 tiles (a 90-degree cone first), switches clockwise on another press, and releases on a 0.4 s hold, the target's fall or flight past 12 tiles, or 2 s out of sight; while locked the stance faces the target and the camera's look-ahead centres between them (0.5 of the way, capped at 5 tiles). Keys: Z, the middle mouse button and the right stick's click (pad R3); **sneak moves on the gamepad from R3 to the d-pad's down, and quick command 1 leaves the pad for the command ring**; heal on H (in the command ring on a gamepad); J opens the journal with the morning report below the current aim. **Changes** S6R-2's defaults | Addendum section 1; R3 is the convention for lock-on, and every pad button was taken | Lock-on on Tab (the command ring's key); no pad binding | 2 (21), 7 (10), 10 (06) |
| DE-4 | **Reach, spacing, lunges and nine weapon movesets.** Every weapon and enemy has a reach; spacing is the heart of a fight. Book 10's "Weapon movesets" gives unarmed (the shove), seax, hand axe, spear (and thrown), sword, Dane axe, club or hammer, shield and hunting bow their reach, speed, frames, damage and type (cut, thrust, blow), shield damage, poise damage, stamina, strength and weakness; "Weapon rules" gives the axe hook (a heavy on a shield opens the guard 30 ticks), the spear past the shield's edge (40%), its push, its weakness inside 0.7 tiles and its 12-tick miss, the sword's +4 parry window, the Dane axe's guard break and shield split, the seax's no-guard-break and cutting, and mail by type (-40% cuts, -25% thrusts and missiles, -10% blows). Lunges cover 1.2 tiles (spear 1.5, Dane axe and club 1.0) for +8 stamina, with a 10-tick tell. The club or hammer is a new weapon class. Each archetype's reach is a combat v2 column (beasts and the dead their own) | Addendum section 1, "Distance and reach" and the weapons table (a proposal, built); its weakness "can't break a guard" is the seax's charged heavy | Damage numbers alone (the addendum asks that choice change how a fight is played) | 1 (09), 2 (12), 7 (01, 07), 10 (06, 07) |
| DE-5 | **Bigger parry rewards, poise and the guard.** The parry window stays 8 ticks (option up to 12; the sword +4). A parry gives 14 ticks of hitstop, a bright spark and a sharp ring, and opens the attacker for 60 ticks (combat v1: 36); the riposte is a heavy, distinct, uninterruptible animation (seax 18 ticks to Dane axe 34) for x3.0 damage (sword x3.5), unblockable and ignoring poise. Poise: 30 ordinary, 60 heavy build, +5 padded, +15 mail; worn by each weapon's poise damage (charged heavies x1.5 and x2.0), refilling at 20 a second after 90 ticks; broken, a 30-tick stagger; broken by a hit of 40 or more, a knockdown (50 ticks and a 20-tick get-up). A block costs 0.6 x the vs-shield damage + 4; a guard break opens the blocker 50 ticks; a shield breaks after 3 guard breaks or one split. **Supersedes** Book 10's parryStagger and blockCost, and section 24's "Light attacks do not stagger a heavy fighter" (now: rarely), once built | Addendum section 1, "Parry, poise and stagger" | A wider window (the addendum keeps it) | 1 (09), 2 (12), 10 (06, 24) |
| DE-6 | **Enemies with self-preservation.** Fighters hold off at the reach they face + 0.4 tiles, circle at strafe speed, step in to strike and out within 20 ticks, feint 10% of the time; wait 0.3 tiles further from a weapon 0.5 tiles longer than theirs, and crowd a seax or bare hands to 0.7 of their reach; below 50% vitality grow cautious (attackGap x1.5, defend 20 points more, back off 1.5 tiles, call once for help) and below 30% fall back towards a friend within 8 tiles; morale still breaks them into flight, pleading or surrender. In groups 1 or 2 press (the S9-29 tokens: 1, warbands 2) while the rest circle 2.5 to 4 tiles out, flank or hang back. They avoid deep water, cliff edges within 1 tile and fire within 1.5, follow no further than 20 tiles from their ground, and remember the steerer for 3 days. Scavengers and thieves leave when the steerer's side within 8 tiles has twice their vitality | Addendum section 1, "Enemies with self-preservation" | | 1 (09), 2 (13), 7 (07), 10 (07) |
| DE-7 | **Strong hit reactions, both ways.** Hitstop is simulation (both fighters' state machines pause; the rest of the world runs; replays are exact): 4 ticks clean (seax 3, club and Dane axe 5), 7 heavy, 10 riposte, 14 parry, 1 on a block. Knockback 2, 4 and 6 px in the hit's direction; a drawn flinch of 8 ticks that does not interrupt; stagger and knockdown by poise; a one-frame flash (never with reduced flashes), directional spray with a decal, sparks on mail and shields; layered sound (flesh, mail or shield wood, and a grunt or cry); camera shake only for heavy hits, ripostes, backstabs and hits of 25 or more on the steerer (none with reduced motion); deaths that vary by weapon and direction and settle naturally. **The steerer's hurt is a sharp breath, never a voiced cry**, so no sound hints at a gender. **Supersedes** Book 10's hitStop 3 and "Hits shake it briefly" (Book 7 section 14) | Addendum section 1, "Hit reactions"; the steerer has no voice (Book 11 section 08) | A voiced cry for the steerer (could imply a gender); hitstop as presentation only (would desynchronise the fight's timing from what is seen) | 1 (09, 17), 2 (12), 7 (01, 12, 14), 10 (06), 11 (07, 08, 12) |
| DE-8 | **Health, stamina, healing and the bars.** Every enemy archetype but the each-uisce gets about 25% less vitality (a combat v2 column in Book 10's Archetypes: the outlaw 90 to 68), and weapon damage is raised so that the first scavengers fall to three clean seax hits (23 each) or one riposte (x3.0); the steerer and residents keep 100. Stamina waits 48 ticks before refilling (combat v1: 36); at 0 the fighter is exhausted until 30 (no dodge, no run, windups and recoveries x1.5). Binding a wound (2.5 s, a binding, +25) and eating (2 s, a carried ration, +10 vitality and +30 stamina) are interruptible by a hit, a dodge, a block, a run or a step, losing the item; up to 5 bindings and 3 rations are carried. **Choice:** 2 bindings lie in the camp's salvage and 3 in Thorkel's chest. Health and stamina are two bars top left, fading 3 s after they are full outside a fight; a thin bar over the locked target and over any enemy for 4 s after it was hit (the addendum's proposal) | Addendum section 1, "Health, stamina and healing"; three seax hits on the v2 outlaw need about 23 damage a hit | Cut health without raising damage (the seax's 14 would need five hits on 68) | 1 (09, 18), 2 (12, 21, 24), 7 (10), 10 (03, 06, 07) |
| DE-9 | **Sneaking, perception and sneak attacks.** Sneak is a toggle for a crouching walk at 1.2 tiles a second (combat v1's 1.75 stays until built); noise by ground (shingle x1.5, sand x0.7, grass x0.5, rock x1.0, water x1.3); tall grass, dune grass and peat banks hide a sneaker beyond 2 tiles, shadow halves sight on one, rain cuts sight to x0.7 and noise to x0.6. Awareness fills by sight (100 a second within 3 tiles, 20 at the edge) and by noise (+30), turns suspicious at 30 ("Who's there?") and alert at 100, and decays after 3 s; marks "?" and "!" are an option, on by default (the addendum's proposal). A backstab (from within 60 degrees of the back, on an unaware or unseeing target) kills outright any foe of combat v2 vitality 75 or less, else does x4 (seax x6), and takes 40 ticks (seax 30); a missile into the unaware does x2 | Addendum section 1, "Sneaking and sneak attacks" | Hold-to-sneak (the addendum says toggle) | 1 (09), 2 (13, 21), 7 (01, 07, 10), 8 (08), 10 (02, 07), 11 (07) |
| DE-10 | **Arrows always register; shields block them; aware enemies sometimes dodge.** A missile sweeps its whole path each tick against every body, shield and piece of cover and hits whatever it touches first, before, during and after a fight, so none passes through anyone (the playtest's bug); a raised shield stops it from the front 120 degrees and it sticks with a thunk; an aware fighter that sees the shooter sidesteps 25% of missiles with a 6-tick tell (never while attacking, blocking, staggered or drawing); the unaware cannot dodge and take x2 | Addendum section 1, "Arrows, shields and dodges", and section 7's second entry | | 1 (09), 2 (12), 7 (01, 18), 10 (07, 21), 11 (07) |
| DE-11 | **Warnings before a fight.** Scavengers, thieves and wary locals warn at 8 tiles, again at 5, and last at 3 ("One more step and you'll bleed."), at least 3 s apart; nearer, or a weapon taken in hand within 8 tiles (after one more warning), starts the fight; beyond 12 tiles they go back to their work; a bargain can end it. Guards of a hostile faction still attack on sight | Addendum section 1, "Warnings before a fight" | | 1 (09), 2 (13), 8 (07, 08), 10 (07), 11 (07) |
| DE-12 | **Checkpoint autosaves**: before each set-piece fight (every encounter with a checkpoint in content, the scavengers at dusk among them), on reaching camp (the first time in a day, under the safe rule) and on waking, so a death costs a couple of minutes, not twenty. **Extends** S9-20 (which had waking and checkpoints before encounters) | Addendum section 1, "Death and saves" | | 1 (09, 15), 2 (20), 3 (Stage 7), 10 (07) |
| DE-13 | **Book 10 keeps combat v1 beside combat v2 until v2 is built.** The tables the built game reads (Weapons, Defence, Fight rules, Vitality, the Archetypes' Vitality column, the Movement row for Sneak, the heavy build's poise row) keep their v1 values and are marked; combat v2's numbers are new tables in sections 06, 07 and 25 and new columns and rows. When combat v2 is built, its tables become the only ones and the v1 rows marked replaced are removed in the same commit, with the parsers moved to the v2 keys. New keys: section 06 `stanceAndLockOn`, `weaponMovesets`, `weaponRules`, `lunge`, `parryAndRiposte`, `poise`, `guard`, `hitReactions`, `healthStaminaAndHealing`, `bars`; section 07 `archetypes` columns "Vitality (combat v2)" and "Reach (combat v2)", `perception` rows surfaceGrass, surfaceRock, surfaceWater, rainFactor and rainNoise, and `awareness`, `sneaking`, `sneakAttacks`, `selfPreservation`, `warnings`, `theScavengersAtDusk`; a new section 25 (`theOpeningGuidanceAndFeel`: `theFirstMinutes`, `guidance`, `worldFeedback`, `doors`, `theWeepingStone`, `musicFades`); new rows in section 03 `workAndTheWreckCamp` (mastStores, shelterRaise, bindingsAtCamp), section 08 `theThreeSearches` (searchesPlaced, keyFromBody, runaSafe, runaTruth, runaLie, rescueGetUp) and section 21 `missiles` (sweptHits, awareDodge). Changed in place, format kept: `startingWeapons.seaxFoundBy` (its wording) and `bowFoundBy` (15 to 30 minutes, the built game's test still passing); only notes changed on `theThreeSearches`' Oddi rows | The fold changes no game code, and the built game and its tests read v1 (the Stage 9 to 12 scenario tests measure fights with those numbers) | Change the v1 values in place (would change the built game's fights and break its measured tests without the v2 rules that make them fair) | 10 (02, 06, 07, 24) |
| DE-14 | **The wreck-scavengers round the new opening.** At the first dusk two of the three, **Svart and Conn**, strip the wreck's stern at the east end of Wreck Strand while **Ketill** watches the sheepfold where Bran holds Thorkel's body. Whoever is neither dead nor held after the dusk fight goes to Ketill, so the fold holds one, two or three; after the fold the living go to their skiff in Seal Cave, as S11-10 has it, and the oar is found if all three are dead. Svart works with his shield on his back and fights the dusk fight with the spear in both hands. **Supersedes** S9-11 (already moved by S10-9) and refines S10-9; S11-9 and S11-10 stand | Addendum section 6 asks for two scavengers at dusk with a friend who runs; Book 4 keeps three at the fold and their skiff in Seal Cave. Ketill holds the fold's parley and is the heavy build (a poor first fight); Conn is the coward who runs; Svart's spear teaches spacing | **The coordinator's alternative**, the third at Seal Cave: rejected, because the fold would then hold only the survivors of the dusk fight (none or one) and Ketill's parley would be gone; new scavengers (new characters and art) | 4 (08), 6 (11, 18), 9 (03), 10 (07) |
| DE-15 | **The first fight, built to teach.** Svart's charged heavy has 12 more ticks of windup and a tell drawn large, and 60% of his attacks are heavies in this fight, so the first parry lands and earns a big riposte with its hitstop; Conn flees to the fold when Svart falls (dead, downed or surrendered) or when Conn is hit; the steerer can instead walk away (they leave at dark), bargain with 2 rd of salt fish from the camp's stores, or sneak through the dune grass on the bank above the stern (tiles 84 to 90 by 29 to 33) for a backstab. 18:30 on day 1 to dark, a checkpoint first. A surrender let go is SPARED and goes back to the band | Addendum section 6, item 4 (a proposal, built) | | 4 (08), 6 (18), 8 (07), 9 (03), 10 (07) |

### Building combat v2 and the arrow fix, 2026-11-12
Choices made while building addendum 2.4 section 1 (DE-1 to DE-15) that the books leave open. Each is built as stated; the flagged ones are for the user.

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DE-35 | **Combat v1's rows go.** With combat v2 built, Book 10 drops the v1 rows DE-13 kept (the v1 Weapons table; Defence's parryStagger, blockCost, guardBreak and turnLock; Fight rules' hitStagger; Vitality's stamina, padded, mail and hitStop rows; the heavy build's v1 poise row), and the Archetypes table keeps one Vitality column (the v2 values) beside Armour, Weapons, Behaviour and Reach. Sneak is 1.2 tiles a second | DE-13 asked for exactly this once v2 is built | Keep both | 10 |
| DE-36 | **The axe hook.** A hand-axe hook pulls a shield aside for 30 ticks: it counts as a guard break for the next blow, and a block in that time costs double | Book 10 says the hook "opens the guard" but not for how long | A one-blow window | 10 (Weapon rules) |
| DE-37 | **Riposte and backstab timings.** Their windup is 40% of the weapon's heavy windup (rows riposteFrames and backstabFrames in Book 10) | Book 10 asks for heavy, distinct, uninterruptible animations without frame counts | The heavy's own windup (too slow to land in the 60-tick opening) | 10 |
| DE-38 | **Footfalls.** A moving steerer makes one footfall of noise every 0.5 s (row footfall), scaled by ground, gait and sneak | Book 10 gives noise by ground but not how often | Continuous noise | 10 |
| DE-39 | **The sidestep.** An aware enemy's sidestep moves 0.6 tiles across the missile's path over 12 ticks (row sidestepMove) and lets that one missile pass; it has a dust puff as its tell | Book 10 gives the chance, not the move | A dodge roll (no frames) | 10 |
| DE-40 | **Warnings.** "Nearer than 3 tiles" is measured from body edge to body edge; stepping within the enemy's reach starts the fight at once; drawing a weapon within 8 tiles gives one more warning (the third line) before the fight | Book 10's warning rows leave the measuring and the order open | Centre to centre | 10 (Warnings) |
| DE-41 | **No endless chases.** A fleeing enemy cornered (by water, a cliff or the zone's edge) surrenders; a cornered archer draws a sidearm once and fights | The Stage 9 tests found fleeing enemies stuck against deep water for ever, and archers stalemated | Flee through water | 10 (Self-preservation) |
| DE-42 | **The backstab arc.** Book 10's "within 60 degrees of a target's back" is read as 60 degrees either side of the back (a 120-degree arc) | Book 9's dune approach to the stern meets Svart side-on while he works; a 60-degree arc in all made the sneak route almost impossible | 30 degrees either side | 10 (Sneak attacks) |
| DE-43 | **The swept box.** A missile's path is swept against each body's box, centred, as tall as the body both ways from its middle; a person lying is hit only by a missile below low-cover height; a dodge no longer passes a missile (only an enemy's sidestep does) | DE-10: every missile registers, in every state | Sampling points (the old bug) | 2 (13) |
| DE-44 | **The stance in the save.** The stance (facing angle, lock target, sneak toggle) lives on the player in the world state (version 11); the stance's facing command (FACE) carries one of 64 directions, so replays are exact | The simulation takes commands only | Facing kept in the scene | 2 (05, 13) |
| DE-45 | **Healing items and the key.** Heal is H (pad: not yet in the command ring, flagged). Bindings are `ITEM_BINDING`, rations `ITEM_RATION`; rations are taken from the camp's stores into the pack on reaching camp, up to Book 10's carried limit; bindings come from salvage and Thorkel's chest (Chapter 1 content) | Book 10 limits healing by carried items without saying how they are carried | A heal menu | 10 (Health, stamina and healing) |
| DE-46 | **Hitstop is a held clock.** Each fighter has a hitstop counter; while it runs, the fighter's state machine and timers are held (its end ticks move on by one each held tick), so its animation holds too; the world runs | DE-7's "simulation, both fighters" made exact | Slowing the whole sim | 2 (13) |
| DE-47 | **Reused frames (flag).** The knockdown draws the downed frames, the sidestep the dodge frames and healing the kneel; riposte, backstab, lunge, parried and get-up have new frames on every body sheet. **A slung shield is drawn on the back:** Svart has a second shield layer, `layer_shield_back_svart` (the same shield on his back in every state and facing, with its layer file `wrecker_svart_slung`), drawn in place of the shield on his arm whenever a fighter has a shield but none in hand and none broken; a split or worn-out shield has no art of its own and is still drawn on the arm | Keeps the art to what reads clearly at 48 px; what is drawn matches the simulation (DE-14: no shield in hand, nothing blocked) | New frames for all eight; a back layer for every shield-bearer (only Svart is ever slung) | 7 (12) |
| DE-48 | **Sounds.** The steerer's hurt sound is a breath, never a voiced cry (DE-7); enemies grunt wordlessly at their own speaking pitch (voice.ts: Ketill 98 Hz, Svart 150, Conn 244); the parry rings, the riposte and backstab are heavy layered hits; the lunge, sidestep, alert, binding and eating each have a sound and a caption | Book 11 asks for layered hit sounds and no voiced cry for the steerer, and voices told apart | One grunt for all | 11 (07) |
| DE-49 | **The dusk fight's interface.** `SCN_SK_DUSK` sets `ch1.dusk` to `FOOD`, `DARK` or `FOUGHT` and `ch1.dusk.<person>` to each scavenger's fate, and emits `DUSK_RESOLVED`; `duskOpen()` says whether it is under way; its checkpoint is `dusk` (on `ENCOUNTER_STARTED`). Walking away and returning before dark finds them still there; after the dusk they go to the fold | The opening's Chapter 1 work reads these | | 6 (18) |
| DE-50 | **Awareness.** A meter from 0 to 100: sight fills it by distance in the cone (halved in cover), noise adds by loudness and fades with distance; it decays when nothing is sensed; SUSPICIOUS at Book 10's 30 turns the head and walks to the last noise; ALERT at 100 or on a hit | Book 10 gives the states and the barks | | 10 (Perception) |
| DE-51 | **Approach.** An enemy closes only when farther than its reach; inside it, it holds, circles or steps out | Fighters stalled at a flank slot just outside striking distance | | 10 (Self-preservation) |
| DE-52 | **Test bots.** The search and repair bots punish a parried or unaware foe, wait for the steerer's own strike to end, and use the oar at 1 tile | Combat v2's commit and reach | | none (tools) |
| DE-53 | **The camp autosave.** Reaching camp autosaves once a day under the reason `CAMP` (a new `AutosaveReason`) | DE-12 | Reuse `REST` | 2 (17) |
| DE-54 | **Exhaustion.** At 0 stamina the steerer is exhausted until stamina is back to Book 10's recoverAt: no run, no dodge (refused with a caption), slowed | Book 10 gives the numbers, not what is refused | | 10 |
| DE-55 | **Older tests retuned.** Stage 9 to 12 tests keep what they check with combat v2 numbers (the hand axe for the axe, the movesets table, the parry's 60-tick opening, guard break on an empty block, the steerer's vitality topped up to 100 in long bot fights); the art test that regenerates every sheet gets 60 s, not 30 (a time limit, not an art threshold) | v1 numbers are gone; the new frames lengthen the regeneration | | none (tests) |
| DE-56 | **The bars.** Health is a red bar above the stamina bar, top left; both fade together once full and out of a fight; the enemy bar is thin, over the locked or recently hit enemy | DE-8 | | 7 (10) |
| DE-57 | **Marks option.** The "?" and "!" marks are on by default and toggled in the system menu (A) or by `?marks=off` | DE-9 asks for a toggle | A settings screen entry (none yet) | 7 (10) |
| DE-58 | **Lock-on leaves with the stance.** Putting the weapon away (out of the stance) releases the lock; the camera then returns to the cursor lead | A lock without a stance has nothing to face | | 10 (Stance) |
| DE-59 | **Shake only from blows.** Screen shake comes only from a heavy, riposte or backstab BLOW (Book 10's shake rows); the blood spray no longer shakes the view | DE-7 "shake only on heavy" | | 7 (14) |

### The final pass (golden saves, checks, captures and the combined report), 2026-11-12
The rework's last session: the golden saves, the full checks, the Build 1 gate's captures and the combined report (`docs/stage-reports/rework-2.4.md`).

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DE-80 | **Golden save 10 is frozen; golden save 11 is the new chapter-end save.** Golden 10 (schema 10) joins goldens 1 to 9: its test loads it through the migration chain to version 11 and plays on, and no longer makes it again from its scenario. Golden 11 (schema 11) is the world of the chapter run, `playChapter('chapter-1')` at the scripted pace (`src/tools/play/chapterRun.ts`): Chapter 1 as 2.4 plays it from waking to the sea map closed, through the new opening, the dusk left alone, the first night's fire, Runa's question answered truly, Bran's search by a fight at the fold (combat v2's first fight), the key from the body, the chest and the bow, the mast stores, the repairs (the oar found once all three scavengers fell), the extended hold, the sea burial and the launch as Grey Seal under a woad raven. A test checks that its scenario reproduces it exactly; `npm run golden` writes only golden 11 | Golden 10's scenario began with the opening that 2.4 replaced, so it can no longer be made again (Book 2 section 20: a golden save that cannot be reproduced is frozen and migrated). The chapter run is the fullest route the bots play and the one whose telemetry the report gives, so the save and the timing are the same world | Golden 10's own route (golden 8 to 10's chain, brought to the 2.4 opening: Bran by a bargain, no fight, no fire scene or Runa's question), which tests less of 2.4; a new seed `golden-11` (the run would not then be the one measured) | none (Book 3 Stage 12's 2.4 note already says the golden saves follow the new opening, the older ones still loading through the chain; Book 2 section 20 keeps a frozen golden save for every version) |
| DE-81 | **Stepped browser tests draw only the last frame of a batch.** In capture mode `oeStep(ms, draw)` runs a frame (the simulation, the scene, the overlay's attributes and captions) with or without drawing it; the stepped helpers (`step`, `stepUntil`, `stepUntilText`, the stepped walk, Stage 7 and 8's own steppers) draw only their last frame. What a test checks is unchanged: every frame still runs | CI's Firefox on macOS draws in software at a few frames a second, and the 2.4 cold open adds about 40 s of game time stepped at 100 ms: the cold open and the rescue specs passed 240 s and failed there (run 43271284179), while everything else passed. Chromium's whole browser suite also went from 7.2 to 4.5 minutes | Longer test limits (Firefox would take over 40 minutes); a shorter cold open in tests (would test less) | 2 (tests) |

| BR-8 | **Firefox no longer blocks publishing, for now.** The deploy job needs only the Linux check (lint, tests, build, Chromium) and WebKit; the Firefox job still runs on every push on macOS, with no continue-on-error, so a failure shows red and each stage report still gives its result. Supersedes BR-7 for Firefox only | The user asked to "just forget about Firefox for a while" (2026-11-12), after GitHub's macOS runners queued for over half an hour and the 2.4 cold open's stepped specs ran past their limits there (fixed in DE-81 but not yet proven) | Keep Firefox blocking and wait on macOS runners | 3 (Stage 12 check 5; the stage reports' CI line) |

Chromium and WebKit are tested and block publishing; the stage reports give their results. Supersedes BR-8 (and BR-2, BR-3 and BR-7 for Firefox) | The user: "I want to forget about Firefox for good" (2026-11-12). A Steam release runs in a desktop wrapper (Electron bundles Chromium; Tauri uses WebView2, a Chromium engine, on Windows and WebKit on macOS and Linux), so Firefox is only ever a browser-only audience; CI's macOS Firefox ran in software at about 4 fps and cost hours | Keep it as a short non-blocking smoke test | 2 (platform), 3 (stage reports and checks) |

## 2026-11-12, Design addendum 2.5 (approved by the user)
Approved by: the user, after a second playtest of the opening and combat v2. Addendum 2.5 wins over 2.4 and everything earlier where they conflict; it is now folded into Books 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8, 10 and 11, and the books are the source of truth again. `docs/design-addendum-2.5.md` is kept as a record only. It replaces two things from 2.4: the light-attack-into-dodge cancel (there are no cancels) and the exhausted rule. No game code changed in the fold; `npm run balance` regenerated `content/balance.json` (revision 2.6). It is built as a second change request after the Build 1 gate and before Stage 13 (DF-20).

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DF-1 | **No animation cancelling.** Every action (attack, parry, block raise, dodge, shove, draw, heal) has wind-up, active and recovery ticks; from the first wind-up tick to the last recovery tick the fighter cannot move, turn, dodge, block, parry or start another action, player and AI alike; nothing cancels into anything (a held charge can only be released; a drawn bow loosed or let down through its recovery); a light pressed in its chain window waits for the recovery's end. **Choices:** the facing is fixed at the first wind-up tick even while locked on (2.5 says no turning); a missed attack adds its move's miss recovery (seax +8 to Dane axe charged +26); a hit on a fighter in a miss recovery, a mistimed parry's recovery, a dodge's recovery or a broken guard is a **punish** (hitstopHeavy and x1.5 poise damage), so a miss leaves the attacker "highly vulnerable"; an action that costs stamina starts only with stamina above 0. **Supersedes** DE-2's attackTurn, dodgeCancel and missRecovery and DE-4's spearMiss (Book 10 "Commitment") | Addendum 2.5 section 1.1 and section 5's first entry | Keep the 4-tick dodge cancel (2.5 removes it); a longer recovery only, with no punish (a miss would cost little more than a hit) | 1 (09, 17), 2 (12), 7 (01), 10 (06) |
| DF-2 | **Grounded movement.** Speed eases up and down: walk 9 ticks to full and 6 to stop, run 24 and 18 (15 from a walk), sneak 12 and 6, back off 12 and 7, strafe 11 and 7, carry 18 and 12, blocking and exhausted at half speed (12 and 6, 15 and 8); x1.25 the ticks in mail or for the heavy build, x1.5 for both; a reversal slows through a stop. Starting any melee attack, a charge included, stops the fighter at once; an attack's only movement is its fixed step over its active ticks (0.1 to 0.45 tiles by move), drawn as a planted step. **Choice:** the lunge keeps its distance (1.0 to 1.5 tiles) as drawn strides during its wind-up, at a fixed distance, so it is not a drift. **Supersedes** Book 10's Charge (heavy) speed 1.5 | Addendum 2.5 section 1.2 and section 5's second entry | Drop lunges (2.4 asked for them and 2.5 does not remove them); a slower charge walk (2.5: movement drops to zero) | 1 (09, 17), 2 (12), 7 (01), 10 (02, 06) |
| DF-3 | **Exhausted at zero stamina.** Any cost that empties the bar (an attack, a parry, a shove, a dodge, a block's drain, running) exhausts: no attack, parry, shove, dodge, block or run, at half speed, until stamina is back to **30% of the current maximum**; regeneration waits 60 ticks from 0 and runs at 20 a second while exhausted (25 otherwise, after 48 ticks; half while blocking), so exhaustion lasts 2.5 s from a full bar. Shown by the exhausted idle and hard breathing, never a word (the refusal word "Too spent" is cut). Everyone but the dead. Climbing, swimming and waist-deep wading keep their own empty-bar rules. **Supersedes** DE-8's exhausted row (until 30; no dodge or run; windups and recoveries x1.5), DE-54 and Book 10's sprintEmpty | Addendum 2.5 section 1.3 and section 5's third entry; the threshold is the coordinator's example | 25% (shorter punish); a fixed time | 1 (09, 18), 2 (12), 7 (01), 8 (10), 10 (06), 11 (07) |
| DF-4 | **The predictive parry.** Wind-up 6 ticks, active 8 (option up to 12; a sword +4), recovery 18 when nothing is parried. **Overlap rule:** a blow is parried only if its first active tick that would meet the parrier falls inside the parrier's active ticks (and arc); a blow that lands in the parry's wind-up or recovery hits. So a parry is pressed 6 to 13 ticks before the blow lands, in the attacker's wind-up. A parry stuns the attacker for parryOpen (60 ticks) after the 14-tick hitstop, and the parrier's remaining ticks become a 6-tick settle before the riposte; a mistimed parry leaves the parrier open (a punish). Missiles, the draugr's grip, the boar's charge and the each-uisce's drag cannot be parried. **Supersedes** Book 10's parryStartup 2, parryWindow and parryRecoveryOnMiss as written; keeps DE-5's rewards | Addendum 2.5 section 1.4 and section 5's fourth entry | A reactive window from the press (2.5 says predictive) | 1 (09), 2 (12), 7 (01), 10 (06), 11 (07) |
| DF-5 | **The slower block.** Raising takes 6 ticks before the guard is live and lowering 6; a raised shield halves speed; each hit drains Guard's blockCost (0.6 x the blow's vs-shield damage + 4: already by the attack's power); a hit that empties the bar breaks the guard with a **60-tick stagger**, knocked back 4 px, then exhausted; the other guard breaks keep their 50 ticks. **Extends** DE-5's guard | Addendum 2.5 section 1.4 | One break time for all (2.5 asks a large stagger for the empty bar only) | 1 (09), 2 (12), 7 (01), 10 (06), 11 (07) |
| DF-6 | **The costly dodge.** 4 ticks of start, a 12-tick dash of 1.25 tiles invulnerable only on its ticks 8 to 13 (6 ticks, the middle), then 18 ticks of planted recovery; 30 stamina (34 ticks in all). **Supersedes** the Defence dodge row (20 stamina, 20 ticks, invulnerable 4 to 13, 1.5 tiles) | Addendum 2.5 section 1.4 | | 1 (09), 2 (12), 7 (01), 10 (06) |
| DF-7 | **AI with intent and distance.** A fight state machine (idle, approach, circle, wait for opening, attack, retreat, recover stamina, flee or surrender) with a least time in each state; fighting choices every 15 ticks (each fighter offset on the `ai` stream; awareness still fills every 6); circle radius the larger of the fighter's attack range and the reach it faces, plus a margin (0.3 to 0.5; wolves 0.8); engage distances, recover-at shares and patience by archetype; an **opening** is a target in any recovery, a broken guard, a stagger, exhausted, healing, drawing or turned away, and a fighter steps in only if it can reach it and finish its wind-up first, judged from what it sees; it retreats after every attack, waits 45 ticks before the next, and turns its circling at most once in 90 ticks. **Choice:** after its patience (e.g. outlaws 2.0 to 3.5 s) it attacks or feints anyway, so two patient sides do not stand off for ever. **Supersedes** decideEvery for fighting and attackGap (Book 10 Fighters' habits); keeps the attack tokens, holdOff as the least circle radius, and the rest of DE-6 | Addendum 2.5 section 1.5 and section 5's fifth entry | Wait for an opening only (a stand-off whenever the player also waits) | 1 (09), 2 (13), 10 (07) |
| DF-8 | **Archers stand to shoot.** An archer shoots from 4 to 7 tiles only with a clear line of sight (no cover, closed door or friend in the way), standing completely still to settle (6 ticks), draw and loose, and recovers 20 ticks; too close (3 tiles) it hops back once (2 tiles over 18 ticks, 25 stamina, not invulnerable), resets for 12 ticks and re-evaluates; cornered (water, a cliff, a wall or the zone's edge within 1.5 tiles behind, or still too close, or too close again within 6 s) it draws its sidearm and fights in melee for the rest of the fight. **Supersedes** fallBackFrom; keeps DE-41's sidearm | Addendum 2.5 section 1.5 | Keep backing away with no limit (archers would kite for ever) | 1 (09), 2 (13), 10 (07) |
| DF-9 | **Read by sight and sound only.** No combat text, damage numbers or log; no caption, word or line reports a hit, block, parry, dodge, damage or state in a fight. Every enemy attack telegraphs in its wind-up: a draw-back of 3, 5 or 7 px by weight, held still for the last 6 ticks of a heavy or charged wind-up; a subtle one-frame flash (two palette steps lighter, never white; a brightened ink edge with reduced flashes) always **28 ticks before the blow lands**, so the parry press comes 15 to 22 ticks after the flash for every attack; a distinct sound at the flash by weight (a sharp breath, steel scraping, a grunt and creak, the charge's creak and ring, a lunge's scuff, the bow's creak; the beasts their own). **Telegraph floors** for an enemy's wind-up: light 28 ticks, heavy 36, charged 48 (the each-uisce 60), an archer's settle and draw 42, an enemy lunge's tell 20; an enemy's light wind-up is lengthened, and a heavy's hold, to meet them. The **awareness marks are off by default** (shown by barks and bodies). **Supersedes** DE-9's and DE-57's "on by default" | Addendum 2.5 section 1.6 and section 5's sixth entry; a fixed flash lead makes the predictive parry a read of rhythm, not of reflexes | The flash at the wind-up's start (its lead would vary by weapon) | 1 (09, 17), 2 (12, 13, 21), 7 (01, 10, 12), 8 (08, 10), 10 (06, 07), 11 (07) |
| DF-10 | **Complete frame data** (Book 10 section 06, "Frame data" and "Enemy attacks"). Every move of every weapon (lights 1 to 3, heavy, charged heavy, lunge, riposte, backstab, the shove and bash, the thrown spear, the bow's draw, loose and recovery) has wind-up, active, recovery, miss recovery, step, stamina, chain window and weight; lights are a little slower than combat v2's (the seax 10/4/14 from 8/4/10, the Dane axe 24/7/32), and the riposte and backstab keep DE-37's ticks. Enemies use the same frames under the telegraph floors (an enemy seax light 28/4/14), string at most two lights (warriors, raiders, huscarls) each with its own telegraph, and the beasts and the dead have their own attacks (the draugr's grip and the each-uisce's drag only dodged). The steerer's own attacks have no floor. **Supersedes** the Windup, Active, Recovery and Chain window columns of Weapon movesets, releaseRecovery, heavySwing, shoveTiming and lungeRecovery | The coordinator's brief asked for full frame data per weapon and per enemy attack; slower lights fit "slower, more methodical" | The steerer under the floors too (every attack 28 ticks or more would feel unresponsive); enemy frames as the steerer's (too fast to parry predictively) | 2 (12), 7 (01), 10 (06) |
| DF-11 | **On-screen text cut back.** At most one information line and one speech line; a new line replaces the old with a 0.15 s cross-fade; never stacked or queued; the same information line suppressed for 20 s, speech by the bark rules; information shows 2.5 s, speech 1 s + 0.05 s a character (2 to 5 s), each fading over 0.4 s; captions bottom centre within the lowest 16% of the view, above the hotbar. In a fight (a hostile alert within 12 tiles, or a blow in the last 4 s) only speech barks and **safety lines** show: the edges and the neck's gale, a horn or the alarm, a supernatural tell, a blade over a downed ally, the steerer down with an ally near, and an arrow from an unseen shooter; one other information line is held and shown after the fight if under 8 s old. Information priority: safety, direction, story, sound. Refusal words stay beside the cursor, one at a time, 1.2 s. **Supersedes** the three stacked subtitles and `captionSeconds` 3 of the built game | Addendum 2.5 section 2 and section 5's seventh entry | Queue lines instead of replacing them (they would still pile up in a fight) | 1 (15), 2 (18), 7 (10), 8 (08, 10), 10 (21), 11 (09) |
| DF-12 | **Tutorial prompts fade.** The move hint, a timed task's press and the pickup tooltips hide (0.5 s fade) while a speech line, story beat, scene or talk shows and come back 2 s after unless done; a prompt is gone for good once done (moving: 2 tiles walked); a tooltip shows 8 s of play or until its controls are used twice. The mouse cursor is not a prompt (DF-15) | Addendum 2.5 section 2 | | 1 (15), 2 (18, 21), 8 (10), 10 (21) |
| DF-13 | **The text audit** (Book 8 section 10): every on-screen message category of the built game, with keep, cut or merge. **Kept:** speech, distant calls, talk and search information, brought home and left behind (held to the fight's end), the safety lines, the pale light (with its direction) and the stone's whisper, the horn and the dispute stingers, the supernatural tells, *They saw you take it*, interaction and tutorial prompts, refusal words, tooltips, the slot's name, the panels, the drawn marks. **Cut:** someone walking up, a foe yielding or running, healing, doors, the opening's actions and moments, the stone's hush and gust, music captions, the other stingers, every combat and missile sound but an arrow from out of sight, every work, camp, repair, rites and launch sound and moment, and "Too spent". **Merged:** Thorkel's shout into his speech line; the alarm drums into the horn; the prompt line's notices into refusal words ("Below the tide line", "No room", "Hands full", "No driftwood", "Too early to sleep"), a refused job into the person's reply, a refused order into a bark. Strings never shown in play (`ui.activity.*`, `ui.work.result.*`) stay unshown | Addendum 2.5 section 2 ("audit every on-screen message and cut any that the picture or sound already conveys") | | 2 (18), 8 (10), 11 (07, 09) |
| DF-14 | **Combat sound captions off, with an accessibility option.** Book 11 section 09 asked for a caption for every gameplay-relevant sound, which 2.5's "no combat text" contradicts. Resolved: in a fight, combat sounds have a drawn twin (the telegraph's draw-back and flash, sparks, the parry's spark) instead of a caption; a display option, **off by default**, shows combat sound captions one line at a time for players who need them. **Flagged for the user** | Addendum 2.5 sections 1.6 and 2 win over Book 11; deaf and hard-of-hearing players keep a way in | No option (would leave those players only the picture) | 2 (18, 21), 8 (10), 11 (09, 12) |
| DF-15 | **The cursor is hidden** in cinematic and non-interactive moments: the cold open (steered with the movement keys), the black, the waking until the first input, and every scene that takes control (the first night's fire, the rites, the launch); it shows again on the first mouse movement once control returns, or when a pointer use is offered | Addendum 2.5 section 3 | | 1 (15), 2 (21), 7 (05), 10 (25) |
| DF-16 | **Lightning, thunder, fog and light in the storm.** Strikes every 6 to 14 s (one scripted to show the skerry about 25 s in), a double flicker (bright 4 ticks, dim 3, bright 3, fading over 0.4 s; never more than two flashes in a second; a single soft swell with reduced flashes) lighting the ship, sea and land to 70% of the day grade with hard shadows; thunder 0.6 to 3 s after by distance, rolling 2.5 to 4 s; two layers of mist drifting downwind at 0.3 and 0.6 tiles a second, 20 to 45% cover, never over the steerer or the oar; the night grade varying 12% either way in drifting patches, and the stern lantern's swinging pool, never a flat blue | Addendum 2.5 section 3; the two-flash limit keeps the lightning under the usual photosensitivity threshold | A single plain flash | 1 (15), 4 (08), 7 (05, 14), 10 (25), 11 (07) |
| DF-17 | **Camera sway, rough water and a pitching ship.** The view sways 3 px up and down with the pitch (3.2 s) and 2 px sideways with the roll (5.4 s), in whole pixels, none with reduced motion; the bow rises and falls 4 px; the hull is drawn in three roll frames; the mast's head sways 2 px and the people brace; swells 3 to 4 tiles apart rolling at 1.2 tiles a second with foam streaks; a wave breaks on the hull every 3 to 6 s in 10 to 18 spray particles 20 to 40 px high; green water every 8 to 12 s; 80 to 120 raindrops slanting 25 degrees. The sounds (thunder, wind, rain, breaking waves, timber rocking with the ship) are Book 11's, with no music and only Thorkel's speech captioned. `AMB_COLD_STORM` is the storm's ambience | Addendum 2.5 section 3 and section 5's last entry | | 4 (08), 7 (05, 14), 10 (25), 11 (07) |
| DF-18 | **Oddi, and every rescued person, a priority fix with a scenario test.** Oddi stayed unresponsive on the sand after his rescue in the second playtest, despite DE-22. The books restate the requirement (Book 2 section 06, Book 5 sections 01 and 02, Book 4 section 08): every rescued person gets up, walks to the camp, works, eats, sleeps under the shelter and answers talk, whatever the hour, route or what the steerer does next. The fix finds the cause, not the symptom. Book 2 section 23 adds the **rescue scenario test**: for every rescued person, the rescue played at morning, midday, dusk and night, by every route home and search choice, with the steerer staying, leaving at once, sleeping or fighting, and across a save and load; each run checks the get-up, reaching camp within `rescueHome` (3 game hours), work, a meal, sleep under the shelter, an answer to talk, and never stuck for `rescueStuck` (30 real seconds). The code fix is being made separately, in parallel with this fold (its tests, `tests/scenario/rescued.test.ts` and `tests/browser/rescued.spec.ts`, cite the build's own decision, DE-82, which its session logs) | Addendum 2.5 section 4 | | 2 (06, 23), 3 (Stage 10), 4 (08), 5 (01, 02), 10 (08) |
| DF-19 | **Book 10 keeps the superseded rows until 2.5 is built**, as DE-13 did: every row the built game still reads and 2.5 replaces stays with its value and a note "Superseded by 2.5, removed when built" (Movement on foot: Charge (heavy); Charged heavy: releaseRecovery; Defence: parryStartup, parryWindow, parryRecoveryOnMiss, dodge; Fight rules: heavySwing, shoveTiming; Running out of stamina: sprintEmpty; Stance and lock-on: attackTurn, dodgeCancel, missRecovery; Weapon movesets' Windup, Active, Recovery and Chain window columns; Weapon rules: spearMiss; Lunge: lungeRecovery; Parry and riposte: parryWindow; Health, stamina and healing: exhausted; Fighters' habits: decideEvery, attackGap, fallBackFrom); Guard's guardBreak and the lunge's tell are kept with a note. When 2.5 is built, the marked rows go in the same commit and the parsers move to the new keys | The fold changes no game code, and the built game and its tests read those rows | Change the values in place (would change the built game's fights without the 2.5 rules that make them fair) | 10 (02, 06, 07, 08, 21, 25) |

| DF-80 | **No combat sound captions, in any mode.** DF-14's accessibility option is withdrawn: a fight is read by sight and sound alone, each combat sound with its drawn twin (draw-back, flash, sparks) | The user: "I don't want any combat sound captions even as accessibility" (2026-11-12) | DF-14's option | 8 (10), 11 (03, 12) |

Supersedes DF-1's "fixed at the press, locked on or not" (which aimed at the cursor) | The user: "Locked on attacks should go in direction of enemy" (2026-11-12) | | 1 (09), 2 (12), 10 (06) |

**Open points for the user** (each folded as decided above unless changed): DF-1's punish (x1.5 poise damage on a fighter caught in a recovery) and the facing fixed at the press while locked on; DF-3's 30% threshold; DF-7's patience, after which an enemy attacks without an opening; DF-9's fixed 28-tick flash lead and the telegraph floors (an enemy seax light now winds up for 28 ticks against the steerer's 10); DF-10's slightly slower lights; DF-14's combat sound captions option, off by default; DF-13's cuts, chiefly the healing, door and opening-action captions.

### Building the rescue fix (Oddi on the sand), 2026-11-12
The cause of DF-18, found by playing the rescue the way the user did. Built as stated; the flagged point is for the user.

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DE-82 | **A rescued person's get-up starts only at the set-down that rescues them.** The cause: DE-22's get-up clock (`rescue.<id>.down`) started at *any* set-down of a rescued person (`wreckSetDown`, `src/sim/narrative/wreck.ts`), but Oddi is rescued only when set down **off his strand** (S10-7: that is what resolves his search and makes him a resident, `searchSetDown`). Set down above the tide line on Wreck Strand, as the opening teaches with Hild, he stood up after 4 s (`stepRescues` made him `idle`) without joining the household: no day ever moved him (only residents have one), talk refused him (`NOT_A_RESIDENT`), and standing he could no longer be lifted (only `down` and `call` lift). The scripted searches always carried him straight off the strand, so no test set him down there. **The fix:** the clock now starts in `rescueSetDown`, called after every other set-down rule, and only for someone of the household who is not downed (Oddi off his strand, Hild above the tide line, a downed ally inside a safe place); set down before that, the person lies (`down`) where put, to be lifted again. **The rescue scenario test** (`tests/scenario/rescued.test.ts`, Book 2 section 23) plays 30 rescues of Hild, Oddi, Bran and Runa through the bots' commands (morning, afternoon, dusk, night; every search's route plus Oddi set down on his strand first, carried to the fire and into the cairn; the steerer staying, leaving at once, going straight to bed or straight to the fold's fight; a save and load straight after, on the walk home, into a new simulation, back to the `search_oddi` checkpoint and to the camp autosave) and follows each for two days (up, at the fire within `rescueHome`, at work, fed, under the shelter at night, talking, never stuck for `rescueStuck`); the stepped browser spec `tests/browser/rescued.spec.ts` plays the playtest's way with the keys. Both fail without the fix. Book 10's `rescueHome` and `rescueStuck` are now parsed (`parseOpeningBalance`). Golden save 11 is regenerated (`npm run golden`): with DE-82 and DE-84 its world holds the same values, only the order in which two facts were set changed. **Flag for the user:** Books 2 and 5 now say no one is left "lying where they were found, waiting to be fetched"; Oddi set down on his own strand still waits to be carried off it, because that is what keeps his dilemma (S10-7, Book 4: "take Oddi home first" costs the silver). The alternative is to count any set-down above the tide line as his rescue (he limps home himself, ODDI_FIRST), which lets the steerer then carry the strongbox off too and so empties the choice unless the scavengers take the silver at that moment | The user's second playtest (addendum 2.5 section 4): find the cause, not the symptom | Rescue him wherever he is set down (changes the search, the user's call); stand him up anyway (the 2.4 symptom fix) | 2 (06, 23), 4 (08), 5 (01, 02), 10 (08) |
| DE-83 | **Hild's walk home is counted from the first fire's smoke.** `rescueHome` (3 game hours from getting up) is measured for Hild from the later of getting up and the first fire, since DE-62 keeps her working on the strand until she sees its smoke; the bot's opening took 3.5 hours from her rescue to the fire | DE-62 and Book 10's rescueHome disagree for Hild | Count from getting up (fails whenever the player takes the camp's first work slowly) | 10 (08) |
| DE-84 | **Out of a fight, a resident walks by their day only.** Found by the rescue scenario test: Bran downed at the fold and carried home kept the fight's last walking goal (`ai.goal`), so each tick the fight walked him towards it and his day walked him back; he hovered half a pixel from his bed (or his work) for ever, standing, never lying down. The fight's walk (`walk` in `src/sim/land/fight.ts`) now leaves a resident alone unless the fight holds them (`heldByFight`: downed, carried, under an order, berserk, carrying, or in an engaged battle) | DE-22: the rescued sleep under the shelter | Clear the goal when the battle ends (every place a battle ends would need it) | 2 (06) |
| DE-85 | **Oddi is rescued wherever he is set down safe** (the coordinator's ruling on DE-82's flag, from the user's "never left where he was found"). Set down anywhere above the tide line, on Wreck Strand or off it, or dropped there by a steerer whose bar runs empty carrying him (any rescued person dropped above the tide line is set down safe: `afterSetDown` in `src/sim/simulation.ts`), he joins the household, gets up after `rescueGetUp` and limps home himself. **The dilemma is kept:** the moment he is set down safe, the wreck-scavengers take the strongbox if it still lies on the strand (`chestTaken`, `src/sim/narrative/searches.ts`): the silver is saved only if it was carried off the strand first. Lifting the strongbox first while he lies there, he says his line `c_oddi.search.chest_saved` ("That's it, take it up. I'll keep. I'm not going anywhere."), once. A new aim `bring_oddi` ("Carry Oddi above the tide line.", content/opening.json, 20 aims now) follows his call, with two hints in his own voice; someone found lying hurt and waiting may speak an aim's hints though not yet of the household (`aims.ts`). **Supersedes** S10-7's "set down outside the strand" and DE-82's lying on the strand (DE-82's cause fix stands: the get-up clock still starts only at a set-down that rescues) | The user: Oddi must never be left where he was found; waiting on the strand reads as stuck | Keep S10-7 (DE-82's flag); rescue him but leave the silver (empties the choice) | 4 (08), 5 (02), 6 (Thorkel's chest), 10 (08) |
| DE-86 | **Interact goes to Hild first while she is caught in the net**: the cut with a blade in hand, or her word that a blade is needed, before Thorkel's chest washed up nearer beside her (`interactFirst` in `wreck.ts`, an optional `first` for `interactionNow` in `onFoot.ts`, used by the simulation and the play view's prompt alike). A click on the chest's own tile still lifts the chest | The obvious action comes first; E lifted the chest instead of cutting the net | Rank by distance only (as built) | none |
| DE-87 | **Hild keeps her footing on the foreshore.** Waiting in the net she follows the tide up and down the shore (`keepFooting`), but no longer onto the tidal flats or the sea as well as rock: at low water in the afternoon she had followed it out to (34, 47), and when the tide turned the flats closed round her and she was out of reach from about 19:45 to 20:30. Caught deeper than the waist by a fast rise she climbs at the knee's pace. Tested every 20 minutes of a day: always on the foreshore with a way to her that needs no deep water | Book 4: she is waist deep among the rocks, to be reached | Close the flats later for her | none |
| DE-88 | **Residents find their way through the zone graph.** `stepResident` (`src/sim/people/residents.ts`) walks to the edge towards the next zone on the shortest chain of links to their place (and comes into view from that side), not only across an edge their zone shares with it; before, someone in view in Seal Cave whose place was at the camp stood still for as long as the steerer watched | Coordinator; found by the rescue scenario test | | 2 (06) |

Book 7 section 08 names four reserved accents | Book 7 section 09 asks for "a reserved accent, kept to this and to nothing ordinary" for the Hag's Stone (DF-21 item 11), and none of the four fits: Rán's sea-light would tie the Cailleach's sign to Rán | Rán's sea-light; keep the pale sea and sky steps | 7 (08, 09) |

Without the context (an old tool) nothing is added, as before | DF-21 item 9; DE-79 left it because a migration could not read Book 10 | A hard-coded count | none |

| DF-53 | **Where the mast stores land (DF-82's tile).** The bundle drops under the lashing and the swell carries it round into the stream's mouth on the outcrop's west side, (37.5, 37.0) of the strand: the one spot off the outcrop's foot that is ankle deep at low water, half tide and high water and reached without a swim at every tide (checked over the height field); it is drawn falling and drifting there over 2.2 s (presentation). It is in view while aiming from the shingle north of the boat | Book 9 section 03 leaves the tile to the build; every spot directly under the lashing is cut off at high water by deep water and the closed flats | A tile under the lashing (dry at low water, unreachable at high); move the mast | 9 (03) |
| DF-54 | **A lower class never cuts a higher one short.** An information line of a lower class arriving while a higher one is still within its hold is dropped, not queued; equal or higher classes replace at once | Book 10 infoPriority speaks of lines "arriving together"; without this a sound would wipe a safety line in its first second | Always replace | none |

### Building committed combat (design addendum 2.5 section 1), 2026-11-13
Choices made while building DF-1 to DF-10, DF-21's combat items and DF-81, which the books leave open. Each is built as stated; the flagged ones are for the user. The superseded Book 10 rows were removed in the same work and the parsers moved to the 2.5 keys (DF-19); `content/balance.json` was regenerated.

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DF-23 | **A held guard is a stance, not an action.** With the shield up and live (after its raise) the fighter may walk at half speed, turn, parry with the shield, bash (the shove) or dodge; a light attack from it waits for the shield's lower (6 ticks). The raise and the lower themselves are locked | Book 10 "Commitment" locks an action from its first wind-up tick to its last recovery tick; the held guard is the block's active phase, which the books let the blocker walk in (blockSpeed) | Lock the whole block (no walking with a shield up, against blockSpeed) | 2 (12), 10 (06) |
| DF-24 | **A feint lowers into a light's recovery.** The AI's feint (Book 10 feint, kept by "AI states") ends the held charge with no swing and no stamina, but the lowering is played as the light's recovery ticks, still locked | 2.5 keeps feints in Attack; a free return to idle would be a cancel in effect | Drop feints (2.5 does not) | 10 (07) |
| DF-25 | **Healing is locked like any action (flag).** Binding a wound or eating cannot be walked, run, dodged or blocked out of (those keys are refused while kneeling); only a hit ends it (a flinch, as before, or worse). Book 10 healInterrupt's step, run, dodge and block cannot happen any more | DF-1 lists heal among the committed actions | Let a step still end it (a cancel) | 10 (06) |
| DF-26 | **An enemy's heavy hold.** Its hold is the larger of the telegraph floor less the swing and the charge level's threshold, so hold and swing together are the "Enemy attacks" row exactly; Svart's svartWindup lengthens his swing at the dusk. The AI decides the hold at the press, so the flash is placed at the floor's lead from the start | Book 10 "Enemy attacks": the rows give the result | | 10 (06, 07) |
| DF-27 | **The household fights at a warrior's distances.** Crew and allies (Bran) use the Warrior row of "Distances by archetype"; Book 10 gives distances for enemy archetypes only | The crew "obey the same rules" (AI decisions, sameRules) and need a row | The Outlaw row (more cautious) | 10 (07) |
| DF-28 | **Decisions fall on a fixed grid.** Each fighter decides on its own grid of decisionInterval ticks (offset 0 to 14 on the `ai` stream); a decision that falls while it is locked in an action is skipped, never moved. Between decisions the state is carried out every tick on the foe's position now. The end of its own attack sends it to Retreat at once (the attack's end, not a choice), with the 45-tick cooldown | "No state change between decisions", and a group never in step | Decide on the first free tick after a lock (the grid drifts and fighters fall into step) | 2 (13) |
| DF-29 | **How long an opening lasts, as a fighter sees it (flag).** A recovery after a miss, a mistimed parry or a dodge, a stagger, a broken guard, a knockdown and getting up, and healing: until its drawn pose ends; exhausted: until the bar is back at the threshold (the bent, blowing idle shows it); a bow drawn: the draw and its recovery; the back turned: the time to turn round. **Consequence for the user:** by takeOpening, an enemy light (28-tick floor) cannot punish a seax light's whiff (22 ticks of recovery and miss recovery); it takes heavier whiffs, a mistimed parry, a dodge's recovery, exhaustion and the like, or attacks when its patience runs out | Book 10 opening and takeOpening, read from the drawn state | Let the AI step in on any whiff (it would be hit as it closes) | 10 (07) |
| DF-30 | **The steerer's exhaustion lives on its fighter.** Running the bar empty (or any cost) exhausts; the simulation makes the steerer's fighter when the bar first empties outside a fight. The sprint's old `winded.run` is gone; a heavy carry and a waist-deep wade still wait for heavyCarryEmpty's 25 (Book 10 "Running out of stamina") | DF-3: running out exhausts as any empty bar does | | 10 (06) |
| DF-31 | **How speed eases (Book 10 "Movement and acceleration").** The velocity is a vector: the part along the way wanted rises at full speed over the ticks to full speed (or falls to the speed wanted over the ticks to stop); the part across or against it falls over the ticks to stop, so a reversal passes through a stop. The mode is the steerer's gait and stance (walk, run, sneak, back off, strafe), carrying, exhausted or blocking; a stop uses the ticks of the mode it was moving in; running from a walk builds over runFromWalk. A wall stops it dead (no stored speed). Residents walking their day (src/sim/people) are not eased (the people work's), and the scripted players ease off the keys near a waypoint | DF-2 | Ease the speed only and turn instantly (a slide round corners) | 2 (12) |
| DF-32 | **The dodge's dash is eased out** as 1.25 x (1 - (1 - k/12)^2) tiles after its k-th dash tick; the start and the recovery stand still. A sidestep and an archer's hop use the same curve over their own ticks | Book 10 dodgeDash: "eased out" | Even speed | 10 (06) |
| DF-33 | **An attack's step carries its reach.** The step forward over the active ticks moves the striker, and a blow meets any body within reach on any active tick, so a light's reach is its weapon's plus its step (seax 0.8 + 0.15 tiles to the near edge) | Book 10 step and reach both stand | Measure reach from where the attack began | 10 (06) |
| DF-34 | **An archer's line of sight** is clear when no blocking ground (rock or a solid tile, a closed door's tile) lies on the line from its bow height to the target and no friend's body stands across it; props as cover are not modelled for the line (they still stop the arrow) | Book 10 lineOfSight; the cover footprints are the missiles' | | 10 (07) |
| DF-35 | **An archer's hop and its corner.** The hop goes straight away from the target over Book 10's 2 tiles and 18 ticks (eased out), when the ground 1.5 tiles behind it (and half way) is open, dry and not kept off, and it is not exhausted; otherwise, or within dashOnce of the last hop, or still within tooClose after the reset, it is cornered: the sidearm is drawn (20 ticks, standing) and it fights from then on at an outlaw's distances. Its hop is drawn with its own frames (`hop`), not the dodge's | Book 10 "Archers"; the task asked for the archer's hop to be drawn | The dodge frames (Book 10 dashAway's note) | 7 (01), 10 (07) |
| DF-36 | **Enemies' second light is not built yet.** "Telegraphs", enemyStrings lets warriors, raiders and huscarls follow with a second light; no Chapter 1 enemy is one, so it waits for Stage 13's warbands | Nothing in Chapter 1 would play it | | 10 (06) |
| DF-37 | **The telegraph's flash is drawn as a glint.** At the flash tick a one-frame highlight (`fx_combat` tell: two palette steps lighter than the iron and cloth it lies on, never white) is drawn along the attacker's drawn-back weapon; with the display's reduced motion only the weapon's edge brightens (the game has no separate reduced-flashes option). The steerer's own attacks have no flash; their tell is the draw-back and a quiet breath | Book 10 flash; Book 7 section 12's telegraph flash | Repaint the whole body for a frame (needs a palette-shift path the renderer lacks) | 7 (12) |
| DF-38 | **The wind-up's frames (flag).** Three draw-back states of two frames (`windup_l`, `windup_h`, `windup_c`: 3, 5 and 7 px back), the second frame the held top; the strike's own cocked frame shows for its last 3 wind-up ticks, then the blow and the follow-through, held through a miss recovery (the 2 extra overbalanced frames of Book 7 section 01 are not drawn). The block's lower plays its raise backwards, and there is no separate block walk (the guard pose is drawn while walking); the walk start and stop and the run start's second frame are not drawn | The frames the task listed first; time | Draw every Book 7 frame now | 7 (01) |
| DF-39 | **Which way the dead fall.** Struck from in front, backwards; from behind, forwards; from the side, or by a club, crumpling. Two frames of the fall (`fall_back`, `fall_fwd`, `fall_crumple`) then the lying dead (a dismembering kill keeps its own corpse) | Book 7 section 12: deaths vary by weapon and the hit's direction | By weapon only | 7 (12) |

### The final pass (the finishing work, the timing, golden save 11, the checks and the report), 2026-11-13
What design addendum 2.5 asked for that the combat build left out (its report's known issues), the cause of the longer chapter, and golden save 11. Each is built as stated; the flagged ones are for the user.

| # | Decision | Reason | Alternatives | Books affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DF-83 | **The finishing frames and the sounds of commitment.** Sounds (Book 11 section 07, "Commitment and stamina"; generated, rendered and measured): the parry's rasp on the tick its wind-up begins, the block's leather creak on the tick the raise begins, the dodge's heavy landing on the tick the dash ends in its planted recovery (a sidestep's landing too), and a whiff's overbalanced scuff at the end of its active ticks (MISSED); a new fight event `COMMIT_SOUND` carries the first three. Each has its drawn twin and no caption (DF-80). Frames (Book 7 section 01; palette-indexed, `people48.py`): a walk start of 2 (the weight tipping onto the planted foot, then a half-stride contact) and a walk stop of 2 (the front foot braking with the arms and hem swinging on, then the feet together); the run start lengthened to 2 (the push-off, then the first drive); the block walk forward, backing off and strafing, 6 frames each at half the stride with the shield (or guard) up; and a miss's overbalance of 2 (pitched over the front foot, the weapon carried low past the line, the back foot dragged up; then the weight caught on a further step), one drawing for every weapon with a spear's own. **Choices:** the overbalance shows over the first 70% of the miss recovery (35% each frame), then the strike's follow-through; the steerer's walk start plays by its ticks from the first moving tick and the stop from letting go of the keys, its last frame held its ticks after the feet stop (a run let go skids on the run stop's first frame); a fighter's start and stop frames are picked by its eased speed against Book 10's walk (start below 40% and 80%, stop above and below 50%), drawing splits like the dodge's and the parried's; every body that fights (the steerer's two, the heavy build, the three scavengers, Oddi and Bran) has them; Hild and Runa gain only the run start's second frame, so every build keeps the same counts (Book 7 section 16); residents walking their day are not eased (DF-31), so they are never drawn starting or stopping. **Supersedes** DF-38's "no separate block walk", "the 2 extra overbalanced frames are not drawn" and "the walk start and stop and the run start's second frame are not drawn". **Flag:** the new frames, for the user's look (`docs/art/rework-2.5/finish/sheets/`) | The combat report's known issues; CLAUDE.md: no polish later | One overbalance per strike (three times the frames for a 2-frame beat) | none |
| DF-84 | **An enemy's second light (Book 10 "Telegraphs", enemyStrings).** A warrior, raider or huscarl (by its archetype in Book 10 section 07) whose first light met its target, struck or blocked, presses again in that light's chain window while it is still in Attack, so a second light follows its recovery with its own wind-up under the telegraph floor and its own flash 28 ticks before its blow; never after a whiff (the miss recovery locks it), a parry (it is stunned) or a light 2, so never a third. No Chapter 1 enemy strings lights (the scavengers fight as outlaws): it is built and tested with Svart set to each of the three archetypes. **Supersedes** DF-36 (not built). **Flag:** the condition, which Book 10 leaves open ("may follow") | Book 10 calls for it, and every stage from Stage 13 assumes 2.5's combat (DF-20) | Follow every first light (also a whiff); a share of the time (Book 10 gives none) | none |
| DF-85 | **The longer chapter was a bot artefact, already fixed.** The 82 min 16 s of DF-70 was measured at commits 34d66b6 and 512afaf, where the scripted players (`openingBot.ts`, `campBot.ts`) eased off the keys near every waypoint of their path, which is every tile, once acceleration came in; they walked most of the way at a fraction of the walk. Commit 5e1df63 let them pass a waypoint where the path runs straight on at speed: from there the chapter takes 61 min 33 s at the human pace and 58 min 38 s at the bot's (291a6c3 the same). Putting the old loop back on 291a6c3 alone gives 82 min 16 s exactly. The 20 minutes were one whole game day: the slow walk pushed the mast stores past dark (20:15 on day 2, from 17:19) and the hull's work to day 6. Real play barely moved: the chest reaches the camp 6 s later than in 2.4 (the carry eases and can end winded), the stores are fetched 1 min 12 s after the bow (1 min 26 s in 2.4; DF-82's shallows), the first rain and the bots' fights change nothing measurable. **Nothing was changed for timing.** Against Book 10's 45 to 60 minutes: 58 min 38 s at the bot's pace, inside; 61 min 33 s at the human pace, 1 min 33 s over (2 min 50 s of it held by menus); the bow at 19 min 10 s and 19 min 55 s, just before bowBy's 20 to 30. **Supersedes** DF-70's "the cause is not isolated" | Telemetry, by commit | Shorten the repairs' waits (cuts content) | none |
| DF-86 | **Golden save 11 is regenerated at schema 12.** `npm run golden` rewrote it from the chapter run once 2.5 was complete (DF-46), so the file named 11 now holds version 12 and loads with no migration; the test checks that and that its scenario reproduces it exactly. Goldens 1 to 10 stay frozen and still load through the step from 11 to 12 | The task and DF-46 | Freeze the old golden 11 and add a golden 12 (Book 2's numbering, but DF-46 said "regenerated") | none |

## 2026-11-13, The user's third playtest
Approved by: the user, in a change request after a third playtest of Chapter 1 (twelve numbered requests: combat 1 to 5, story and guidance 6 to 8, interface 9 and 10, a bug 11, a developer menu 12, and tests), with two more added during the fold: a sneak-key hint and Bran and the scavengers talking at the fold. The user's words win over everything earlier. Folded straight into the books (there is no addendum this time): Books 1 to 11, `content/balance.json` (regenerated), `content/strings/en.json` and `content/ids.json`. No game code was changed. Every row below is built as stated unless the user changes it; the open points follow the table.

| DG-1 | **A third change request before Stage 13, and the bookkeeping.** Built as the third Build 1 rework, like 2.4 and 2.5 (DE-33, DF-20): it changes what Stages 5, 6, 7, 9, 10, 11 and 12 deliver, its checks are theirs run again with Book 2 section 23's new tests, new art starts as sheets or mockups for review, and it stops for the user's playtest. Book 1 is at revision 2.7 (so `content/balance.json` says 2.7). From Stage 13 every new enemy, weapon, place and fight follows these rules (Book 3 section 01) | The request is a playtest's change list; Book 3's pattern for those | A new stage | 1, 3, 10 |
| DG-2 | **Finishing a beaten enemy: the choice and its keys.** A foe is beaten when downed, begging (pleading or surrendered), or staggered at or below 30% of its vitality (`beatenBelow`); such a stagger becomes a 90-tick reel. Within 1.2 tiles, in the strike arc and out of an action, the steerer may **hold V** (the finisher, `finisherHold` 24 ticks) to finish or **press G** (a new action, let go) to spare. On a gamepad, while the choice is offered and no other foe within reach is winding up, Y held finishes and A lets go; otherwise they parry and interact. The hold is a choosing (no stamina, no lock, ended by a hit); the finisher then commits. A finisher kills outright and writes `SURRENDERED_KILLED` on the downed and begging, `KILLING` on the reeling. **Extends** Book 2's finishing blows (downed and surrendered only) | Request 1. "Staggered" read as a beaten stagger: offering a kill on every poise break at full health would end every fight in one stagger | Every stagger; a press, not a hold (accidental kills) | 1 (09), 2 (12, 21), 6 (16), 10 (06) |
| DG-3 | **Finishers by weapon, with weight and hitstop, never torture.** Two variants per weapon (standing, for a reeling or kneeling foe; lying, for a downed or prone one) for the seax, hand axe, spear, sword, Dane axe, club and unarmed; unarmed, the steerer takes up the foe's own weapon and strikes once with it. Each has wind-up, active and recovery ticks, a step and a hitstop of 10 to 16 ticks, with the riposte's shake (Book 10 "Finishers"); 6 to 8 frames each (Book 7 section 01). One blow, no second, no slow motion, no kill camera, nothing drawn out | Request 1; Book 1's ban on player torture | A strangle for unarmed (lingering); one finisher per weapon (the user asked for several) | 1 (09, 17), 2 (12), 7 (01, 12), 10 (06), 11 (07) |

Due 10 to 40 days later with a precursor 3 days before. An ambush comes on the next road leg, route or strand at night, with 1 to 3 others, its sign and their line first. ### Building the interface, the stuck spot and the developer menu (the user's third playtest), 2026-11-13
Built: DG-15 to DG-21 (the pause menu, the slots, reset to the start of the day, settings, controls and fullscreen, the intro's skip, no trap and the developer menu). Report: [playtest-3-interface.md](stage-reports/playtest-3-interface.md).

| DG-70 | **The stuck spot's cause, found by replaying the place (DG-20).** The steerer stood on the tidal flats at the western outcrop's seaward foot, by the mast and below the lashing, while the low-water window was open (day 1 to about 19:30); when it closed, the flats under the footprint became blocking ground, and since every step tests the whole footprint (`footprintClear`), every step in every direction was refused: frozen in the water at 20:41. The edge-walk test then found the same cause's second form: a dry hump inside a flats tile (the drawn rock's height reaches over it) that the rising flood rings with deep water, which the depth rule never lets anyone enter; 17 such points on Wreck Strand (three of them at the mast's foot) and in Seal Cave. **Fix, at the cause** (`stepClear`, `src/sim/land/onFoot.ts`): tideClosing as Book 10 writes it (closed tidal ground under a body is open to it, and its water, however deep, is wading water, until it leaves the flats) and neverFrozen (a body overlapping blocking ground, closed doorways included, may move any way that does not deepen the overlap). A step the old rule allowed is allowed still, so golden save 11's scenario is unchanged by it (checked on a clean copy of the tree) | Book 10 "No trap"; the user's screenshot; Book 9 section 13 ("a plan that fails is changed, never the test") | Widen or rock over 17 tiles of the plans (the bake ties heights to the drawn terrain, not the tiles, so the humps would move, not go); push the steerer out when stuck (hides the cause, DG-20) | 2 (10), 10 (25) |
| DG-71 | **Deep water's pace** is the waist band's (0.5 of the wade), for whoever is in it (the flood risen round them). Deep has no pace of its own in Book 10, and the old code moved at the full wade there | A pace was needed for the caught; the deepest wading band's is the book's nearest | Full wade speed (faster than waist deep); refuse moving in deep (traps) | 10 (02) |
